### Tool for Supporting Debugging and Understanding of Normative Requirements Using LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05504v1)
- **Authors**: Alex Kleijwegt, Sinem Getir Yaman, Radu Calinescu
- **Abstract**: Normative requirements specify social, legal, ethical, empathetic, and cultural (SLEEC) norms that must be observed by a system. To support the identification of SLEEC requirements, numerous standards and regulations have been developed. These requirements are typically defined by stakeholders in the non-technical system with diverse expertise (e.g., ethicists, lawyers, social scientists). Hence, ensuring their consistency and managing the requirement elicitation process are complex and error-prone tasks. Recent research has addressed this challenge using domain-specific languages to specify normative requirements as rules, whose consistency can then be analyzed with formal methods. Nevertheless, these approaches often present the results from formal verification tools in a way that is inaccessible to non-technical users. This hinders understanding and makes the iterative process of eliciting and validating these requirements inefficient in terms of both time and effort. To address this problem, we introduce SLEEC-LLM, a tool that uses large language models (LLMs) to provide natural-language interpretations for model-checking counterexamples corresponding to SLEEC rule inconsistencies. SLEEC-LLM improves the efficiency and explainability of normative requirements elicitation and consistency analysis. To demonstrate its effectiveness, we summarise its use in two real-world case studies involving non-technical stakeholders.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SLEEC-LLM, a tool designed to assist non-technical stakeholders in debugging and understanding inconsistencies in normative requirements specified in SLEEC DSL. SLEEC-LLM leverages large language models (LLMs) to translate complex model-checking counterexamples into natural language explanations and provide suggestions for resolving conflicts within SLEEC rule sets. The tool's architecture involves prompt engineering, incorporating SLEEC rules, formal semantics in CSP, and counterexamples to generate human-readable interpretations and resolution suggestions. A user study demonstrated that SLEEC-LLM improves efficiency and explainability, reducing the time and iterations required to resolve rule conflicts.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its application of LLMs to improve the usability of formal verification results for non-technical users in the context of SLEEC (Social, Legal, Ethical, Empathetic, Cultural) requirements.  While LLMs are increasingly used in software engineering, their application to bridge the gap between formal methods and domain experts in normative requirements elicitation is a worthwhile contribution.  Existing research focuses on either formal methods for consistency validation or LLMs for requirements engineering in general. This paper combines both.
*   **Significance:** The significance of the work stems from the challenges inherent in eliciting and validating normative requirements. These requirements are complex, often ambiguous, and involve stakeholders with diverse backgrounds. SLEEC-LLM has the potential to reduce errors, improve understanding, and streamline the requirements engineering process. The user study provides preliminary evidence of these benefits, showing a considerable reduction in both time and iterations required to resolve conflicts. The approach facilitates a more collaborative and efficient requirements elicitation process.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addresses a practical problem:** It targets a real and pressing challenge in requirements engineering: how to bridge the gap between formal methods and non-technical stakeholders.
    *   **User Study:**  The inclusion of a user study, even with a small sample size, provides valuable empirical evidence for the tool's effectiveness.
    *   **Integration of Formal Methods and LLMs:** The approach combines the rigor of formal methods with the interpretability of LLMs, offering a potentially powerful solution.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited User Study:** The user study involved a limited number of participants and case studies. A more extensive evaluation with diverse stakeholders and a wider range of SLEEC rule sets would strengthen the findings.
    *   **LLM Reliance:**  The tool's effectiveness is heavily dependent on the quality and accuracy of the LLM. Potential issues include bias, hallucination, and misinterpretation of the formal semantics. There's limited discussion on how to mitigate these risks.
    *   **Scalability:**  The paper does not explicitly address the scalability of the approach to larger and more complex SLEEC rule sets.
    *   **Threats to Validity:** The paper briefly mentions but does not thoroughly discuss threats to validity. Potential issues include construct validity (measuring the correct constructs) and internal validity (controlling for confounding variables during the user study). Furthermore, user's complete reliance on LLM suggestion without critically reviewing is a threat.
*   **Potential Impact:**  The tool has the potential to influence the way SLEEC requirements are elicited, validated, and managed. By making formal verification results more accessible, it could lead to more robust and reliable systems that adhere to ethical, legal, and social norms.
* **Improvements**:  The tool performance could be improved by adding explanations of the error category that includes the deadlock. Furthermore, LLMs can also generate the test cases to check for inconsistencies in the rules.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the above points, a score of **6** is warranted. While the paper addresses a significant problem and presents a novel approach with promising preliminary results, it also has limitations in terms of the scope of the user study, reliance on LLM accuracy, scalability, and an exploration of potential ethical implications. A more rigorous evaluation and discussion of the potential limitations would have increased the score.

### Disappearing Ink: Obfuscation Breaks N-gram Code Watermarks in Theory and Practice
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05512v1)
- **Authors**: Gehao Zhang, Eugene Bagdasarian, Juan Zhai, Shiqing Ma
- **Abstract**: Distinguishing AI-generated code from human-written code is becoming crucial for tasks such as authorship attribution, content tracking, and misuse detection. Based on this, N-gram-based watermarking schemes have emerged as prominent, which inject secret watermarks to be detected during the generation. However, their robustness in code content remains insufficiently evaluated. Most claims rely solely on defenses against simple code transformations or code optimizations as a simulation of attack, creating a questionable sense of robustness. In contrast, more sophisticated schemes already exist in the software engineering world, e.g., code obfuscation, which significantly alters code while preserving functionality. Although obfuscation is commonly used to protect intellectual property or evade software scanners, the robustness of code watermarking techniques against such transformations remains largely unexplored. In this work, we formally model the code obfuscation and prove the impossibility of N-gram-based watermarking's robustness with only one intuitive and experimentally verified assumption, distribution consistency, satisfied. Given the original false positive rate of the watermarking detection, the ratio that the detector failed on the watermarked code after obfuscation will increase to 1 - fpr. The experiments have been performed on three SOTA watermarking schemes, two LLMs, two programming languages, four code benchmarks, and four obfuscators. Among them, all watermarking detectors show coin-flipping detection abilities on obfuscated codes (AUROC tightly surrounds 0.5). Among all models, watermarking schemes, and datasets, both programming languages own obfuscators that can achieve attack effects with no detection AUROC higher than 0.6 after the attack. Based on the theoretical and practical observations, we also proposed a potential path of robust code watermarking.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the robustness of N-gram-based watermarking techniques for code generated by large language models (LLMs) against code obfuscation. The authors demonstrate, both theoretically and empirically, that these watermarking schemes are vulnerable to code obfuscation techniques. They model code obfuscation as a Markov random walk and prove that with the satisfaction of a specific assumption called "distribution consistency", it's impossible for N-gram-based watermarking to remain robust. Extensive experiments using state-of-the-art watermarking schemes, LLMs, programming languages, code benchmarks, and obfuscators show that obfuscation drastically reduces the detection abilities of watermarking schemes to near random guessing. The paper also touches on potential future research paths, specifically the idea of more semantically aware watermarking.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its rigorous evaluation of code watermarking schemes against *code obfuscation*, a sophisticated attack that has been largely overlooked by previous research. Prior work often considered simpler defenses or code transformations. Modeling the obfuscation process mathematically provides a significant contribution, and this level of formalization is not commonly seen in evaluations of code watermarking techniques. However, the general concept of attacking watermarks isn't new; it's the *method* of attack and its formal analysis that distinguishes this work.
*   **Significance:** This paper has significant implications for the field. If code generated by LLMs is to be reliably identified and attributed, current N-gram-based watermarking techniques are insufficient. The finding is important for copyright protection, plagiarism detection, and software vulnerability monitoring in the age of AI-generated code. It pushes the community towards developing more robust, potentially semantically-aware watermarking methods. The results might influence policy discussions surrounding AI-generated content. The study also identifies a theoretical limitation, pointing out the necessity of considering more semantics-aware watermarking strategies.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Rigorous methodology:** The combination of theoretical analysis and extensive experimental validation is a key strength. The mathematical model adds credibility to the empirical findings.
    *   **Comprehensive experiments:** The experiments cover a wide range of watermarking schemes, LLMs, programming languages, benchmarks, and obfuscators, lending strong support to the paper's conclusions. The use of well-established, off-the-shelf obfuscators provides realism to the attack scenarios.
    *   **Clear presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the theoretical framework, experimental setup, and results.
    *   **Well-defined attack model:** Providing a formal model for code obfuscation as an attack is essential for understanding the limitations of the current watermarking strategies.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Assumption of "distribution consistency":** The theoretical proof hinges on the assumption of "distribution consistency." While the authors provide experimental evidence for this assumption, it may not hold in all cases. If the obfuscation changes the distribution considerably, then their analysis is weakened. It would have been even stronger if they had clearly identified the types of scenarios and obfuscators for which this assumption will and will not hold.
    *   **Limited to N-gram watermarking:** While focusing on N-gram-based methods makes the analysis tractable, it might not generalize to all possible watermarking techniques. The paper acknowledges this limitation, but a brief discussion of alternative watermarking paradigms and their potential robustness would have been beneficial.
    *   **Future research directions are preliminary:** The potential path towards robust watermarking (semantically-aware) is only briefly sketched and could be developed more substantially.
*   **Potential Impact:**
    *   The paper serves as a wake-up call to the code watermarking community, highlighting the vulnerabilities of prevailing techniques.
    *   It will likely stimulate research into more resilient watermarking methods.
    *   It could contribute to the development of guidelines for responsible use of LLMs in code generation.
    *   It raises awareness of potential avenues to evade content detection mechanisms.

**Score Justification:**

The paper presents a significant negative result for a prominent approach to code watermarking. Its rigorous methodology, comprehensive experiments, and formal analysis make a compelling argument that N-gram-based watermarking is not robust against code obfuscation. The paper's weaknesses (primarily the reliance on the "distribution consistency" assumption and limited exploration of future directions) prevent it from achieving a higher score.

### Empowering Healthcare Practitioners with Language Models: Structuring Speech Transcripts in Two Real-World Clinical Applications
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05517v1)
- **Authors**: Jean-Philippe Corbeil, Asma Ben Abacha, George Michalopoulos, Phillip Swazinna, Miguel Del-Agua, Jerome Tremblay, Akila Jeeson Daniel, Cari Bader, Kevin Cho, Pooja Krishnan, Nathan Bodenstab, Thomas Lin, Wenxuan Teng, Francois Beaulieu, Paul Vozila
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) such as GPT-4o and o1 have demonstrated strong performance on clinical natural language processing (NLP) tasks across multiple medical benchmarks. Nonetheless, two high-impact NLP tasks - structured tabular reporting from nurse dictations and medical order extraction from doctor-patient consultations - remain underexplored due to data scarcity and sensitivity, despite active industry efforts. Practical solutions to these real-world clinical tasks can significantly reduce the documentation burden on healthcare providers, allowing greater focus on patient care. In this paper, we investigate these two challenging tasks using private and open-source clinical datasets, evaluating the performance of both open- and closed-weight LLMs, and analyzing their respective strengths and limitations. Furthermore, we propose an agentic pipeline for generating realistic, non-sensitive nurse dictations, enabling structured extraction of clinical observations. To support further research in both areas, we release SYNUR and SIMORD, the first open-source datasets for nurse observation extraction and medical order extraction.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the under-explored yet high-impact clinical NLP tasks of structured reporting from nurse dictations and medical order extraction from doctor-patient consultations.  It evaluates both closed- and open-source LLMs on these tasks using private and newly released open-source datasets.  The authors release two new datasets: SYNUR, a synthetic nursing dataset for observation extraction, and SIMORD, a dataset for medical order extraction.  The work evaluates model performance, identifies challenges related to long contexts, flowsheets, and spoken language interpretation, and demonstrates the feasibility of using LLMs to reduce documentation burden on healthcare providers. The authors also propose an agentic data generation pipeline for producing realistic, non-sensitive synthetic data.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in releasing the SYNUR and SIMORD datasets. While the tasks themselves (nurse dictation structuring and medical order extraction) are known, the lack of publicly available datasets has hindered research. The agentic pipeline for generating synthetic nurse dictations is a valuable contribution.  The application of LLMs to these specific tasks is not inherently novel, as LLMs are increasingly applied across NLP. However, focusing on these practical clinical applications, and the systematic evaluation with both closed and open-source models, provides tangible value.
*   **Significance:** The potential impact is significant. Reducing documentation burden for healthcare providers has the potential to improve efficiency and allow for greater focus on patient care. The release of SYNUR and SIMORD can spur further research and development in these areas. The benchmarking of different models provides a useful baseline for future work.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Dataset Creation:** The creation of the SYNUR dataset with its agentic generation pipeline addresses a key challenge: data scarcity in sensitive clinical domains. The SIMORD dataset is also a valuable addition.
    *   **Practical Focus:** The work focuses on real-world clinical needs, increasing its relevance and potential for adoption.
    *   **Model Evaluation:** The paper provides a reasonably thorough evaluation of different LLMs, including open- and closed-source models.
    *   **Challenge Identification:** The paper identifies and discusses key challenges in these tasks, such as handling long contexts and interpreting spoken language.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Exploration of Open-Source Models:** While the paper tests both closed- and open-source models, the open-source evaluation is somewhat limited. More in-depth exploration and optimization of open models would strengthen the findings.
    *   **Generalizability of Synthetic Data:** A potential concern is the generalizability of SYNUR, given that it's a synthetic dataset. While the authors tried to ensure realism, synthetic data may not fully capture the complexities of real clinical language. Further evaluation with real-world data is crucial.
    *   **Depth of Clinical Integration Discussion:** The paper could benefit from a deeper discussion of how these solutions would integrate into existing clinical workflows and address practical considerations beyond technical performance.
    *   **Impact Assessment:** While the paper discusses potential impact, it lacks a quantitative assessment of the actual reduction in documentation burden or improvement in efficiency. Such data would make the case stronger.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by providing resources (datasets) and benchmarks for future research in clinical NLP. The agentic pipeline for synthetic data generation could be adopted and extended by other researchers.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a solid contribution by addressing practical clinical NLP tasks and releasing valuable datasets. While the core NLP techniques are not revolutionary, the targeted application and the resource creation are significant. The evaluation is reasonably thorough but could be strengthened with deeper exploration of open-source models and a more quantitative assessment of impact. The paper's practical focus and resource creation give it real-world relevance. However, the limitations regarding synthetic data generalizability and workflow integration hold it back from being a truly exceptional contribution.

### Conversational Education at Scale: A Multi-LLM Agent Workflow for Procedural Learning and Pedagogic Quality Assessment
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05528v1)
- **Authors**: Jiahuan Pei, Fanghua Ye, Xin Sun, Wentao Deng, Koen Hindriks, Junxiao Wang
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have advanced virtual educators and learners, bridging NLP with AI4Education. Existing work often lacks scalability and fails to leverage diverse, large-scale course content, with limited frameworks for assessing pedagogic quality. To this end, we propose WikiHowAgent, a multi-agent workflow leveraging LLMs to simulate interactive teaching-learning conversations. It integrates teacher and learner agents, an interaction manager, and an evaluator to facilitate procedural learning and assess pedagogic quality. We introduce a dataset of 114,296 teacher-learner conversations grounded in 14,287 tutorials across 17 domains and 727 topics. Our evaluation protocol combines computational and rubric-based metrics with human judgment alignment. Results demonstrate the workflow's effectiveness in diverse setups, offering insights into LLM capabilities across domains. Our datasets and implementations are fully open-sourced.
- **Summary**: **Summary:**

The paper introduces WikiHowAgent, a multi-agent workflow leveraging Large Language Models (LLMs) to simulate interactive teaching-learning conversations for procedural learning and pedagogic quality assessment. The workflow integrates teacher and learner agents, an interaction manager, and an evaluator. A dataset of 114,296 teacher-learner conversations grounded in 14,287 tutorials across 17 domains and 727 topics is presented. The paper combines computational and rubric-based metrics with human judgment to evaluate the workflow's effectiveness, demonstrating insights into LLM capabilities across domains. The datasets and implementations are open-sourced. The study also evaluates the models based on domain, and analyzes human judgments against the LLM-generated metrics.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper presents a valuable and well-executed exploration of LLMs in educational settings. The development of WikiHowAgent, a multi-agent framework for simulating teaching and learning, is novel. The integration of teacher, learner, evaluator, and interaction manager creates a sophisticated system that moves beyond single-agent approaches.

**Strengths:**

*   **Dataset:** The generation and use of a large-scale, diverse dataset based on WikiHow tutorials is a significant strength. This dataset allows for comprehensive evaluation of LLM capabilities across multiple domains and topics.
*   **Multi-agent Workflow:** The proposed multi-agent architecture is innovative. It simulates the dynamic interaction between teachers and learners, which can be a crucial aspect of effective education.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The evaluation protocol combines computational metrics (BLEU, METEOR, etc.), rubric-based metrics (Clarity, Truthfulness, Engagement, etc.), and human judgment. This multi-faceted approach provides a robust assessment of the system's performance.
*   **Open-Sourcing:** The open-sourcing of datasets and implementations facilitates further research and development in this area.
*   **Performance analysis:** The study considers multiple domains and models and analyzes the results which allows for better generalization than a focused study.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Simulated Learners:** The learner agents are still simulated. While the framework provides insights into LLM capabilities, the interactions might not fully capture the nuances and complexities of real human learners. Incorporating human-in-the-loop learners would significantly improve the realism and validity of the results.
*   **Limited User interaction during evaluation:** While human assessment is discussed, user studies with human learners are lacking in the study. The impact of the tool on real learners is unconfirmed.
*   **Topic Complexity:** The paper acknowledges that topic complexity and data distribution could influence performance. While domain-level analysis is performed, a more fine-grained analysis at the topic level might reveal additional insights.
*   **Metric Discrepancies:** The paper reports discrepancies between human and LLM-based evaluation metrics, particularly for aspects like Depth, Engagement, and Naturalness. This misalignment indicates a need for improved LLM-based evaluation strategies.
*   **Limited novelty:** The multi-agent approach is novel, but it's a fairly incremental improvement that doesn't provide a particularly high degree of surprise or counterintuitiveness, which is typical for high-impact contributions.

**Significance:**

The paper contributes to the growing body of research on LLMs in education and sets a strong foundation for future research. The WikiHowAgent framework and associated dataset provide valuable resources for researchers and developers interested in creating scalable, AI-powered tutoring systems. The detailed evaluation protocol and analysis offer important insights into the strengths and weaknesses of LLMs in educational contexts. By highlighting the need for improved learner engagement and evaluation strategies, the paper points toward important directions for future research.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper makes a significant contribution to the field by presenting a novel, multi-agent framework for simulating teaching-learning interactions and provides a large dataset to advance the educational applications of LLMs. The comprehensive evaluation approach strengthens the paper's findings. However, the reliance on simulated learners and the limited depth of analysis in certain areas (such as topic complexity) detract from the paper's overall impact. The discrepancies observed between human and LLM judgments also suggest that more work is needed to refine the evaluation process and to better align automated evaluations with human perception. Therefore, while the paper offers valuable insights and resources, its limitations warrant a score of 7, indicating a strong but not exceptional contribution.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### SenseCF: LLM-Prompted Counterfactuals for Intervention and Sensor Data Augmentation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05541v1)
- **Authors**: Shovito Barua Soumma, Asiful Arefeen, Stephanie M. Carpenter, Melanie Hingle, Hassan Ghasemzadeh
- **Abstract**: Counterfactual explanations (CFs) offer human-centric insights into machine learning predictions by highlighting minimal changes required to alter an outcome. Therefore, CFs can be used as (i) interventions for abnormality prevention and (ii) augmented data for training robust models. In this work, we explore large language models (LLMs), specifically GPT-4o-mini, for generating CFs in a zero-shot and three-shot setting. We evaluate our approach on two datasets: the AI-Readi flagship dataset for stress prediction and a public dataset for heart disease detection. Compared to traditional methods such as DiCE, CFNOW, and NICE, our few-shot LLM-based approach achieves high plausibility (up to 99%), strong validity (up to 0.99), and competitive sparsity. Moreover, using LLM-generated CFs as augmented samples improves downstream classifier performance (an average accuracy gain of 5%), especially in low-data regimes. This demonstrates the potential of prompt-based generative techniques to enhance explainability and robustness in clinical and physiological prediction tasks. Code base: github.com/anonymous/SenseCF.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "SenseCF: LLM-Prompted Counterfactuals for Intervention and Sensor Data Augmentation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SenseCF, a framework that uses Large Language Models (LLMs), specifically GPT-4o, to generate counterfactual explanations (CFs) for machine learning predictions based on clinical and sensor data.  The generated CFs are then used both for understanding what minimal changes are required to alter a model's prediction (interpretability) and as augmented data to improve model robustness and address data imbalance. The paper compares SenseCF with traditional CF generation methods (DiCE, CFNOW, NICE) on two datasets – AI-READI (stress prediction) and a heart disease dataset.  The results show that SenseCF achieves high plausibility and validity and demonstrates improvements in downstream classifier performance when the generated CFs are used for data augmentation, particularly in low-data scenarios.  The approach avoids the need for model internals or gradient access, making it more generally applicable.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The primary novelty lies in the application of LLMs, especially GPT-4o in a zero-shot/few-shot prompting setup, for generating counterfactual explanations specifically for sensor-derived and clinical data. While LLMs have been used for counterfactual generation in other domains (mainly NLP), their rigorous evaluation and application to the more structured, numerical, and multimodal context of healthcare data is a worthwhile contribution. Specifically, using LLMs for *actionable intervention* points is a novel angle. The paper also highlights the novel concept of augmenting data using LLM-generated data in an innovative and impactful way.
Using *off-the-shelf* LLMs, specifically GPT-4o for this particular task is a valid step, but it's important to note that it requires paying for inference for each prompt. The *novelty* of using this model is limited to simply using the newer version of OpenAI's model compared to other LLM-based CFE methods.

**Significance:**

The paper addresses an important problem: improving the interpretability and robustness of ML models in healthcare, where actionable insights are crucial. The fact that SenseCF does not need model internals or gradient access significantly enhances its applicability.  The improvement in classifier performance through data augmentation using LLM-generated CFs has the potential to improve prediction and intervention strategies in low-data clinical settings. This could have a tangible impact on healthcare outcomes. The focus on preserving domain constraints (e.g., immutable features like age/sex) further enhances the real-world applicability of the generated CFs.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear problem definition and motivation:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges of interpretability and robustness in healthcare ML and how CFs can address these.
*   **Rigorous evaluation:** The paper employs standard metrics (validity, plausibility, sparsity, distance) to compare SenseCF against established baselines. The evaluation on two distinct datasets strengthens the results.
*   **Actionable counterfactuals:** The method focuses on generating counterfactuals that are practically useful, considering domain-specific constraints.
*   **Data Augmentation:** A strong aspect is demonstrating the impact on downstream tasks via data augmentation.
*   **Clear code base:** Provides access to the code, which is a good practice.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Dependency on LLM Quality and Cost:** The quality of the generated CFs is heavily dependent on the capabilities of the LLM (GPT-40). This introduces a reliance on a specific model and potential cost implications due to API usage. There's a potential limitation on the scalability of the method (especially in a deployment setting) given the API cost and LLM's inference time. This cost factor isn't really discussed.
*   **Lack of comparison to other LLM-based CFE methods:** The baselines don't include comparisons to recent LLM-based methods for CFE in structured settings.
*   **Generalizability and scalability:** While the two datasets provide some evidence, it would be important to understand how the performance scales to other datasets and domains.
*   **LLM prompt engineering discussion**: A much deeper discussion on the impact of different prompt engineering techniques and strategies, and their effect on counterfactual explanations is required. The template provided (Figure 3) is rather basic, and lacks the sophistication and techniques that could be applied.
*   **Limited Interpretability of Feature Diversity Graph:** Interpretation of the feature diversity graph (Figure 4) could be improved. While it's shown, it is not deeply analyzed as to *why* the 3-shot approach results in larger step counts.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper makes a valuable contribution by applying LLMs to generate actionable counterfactuals for clinical and sensor data, several weaknesses need to be addressed. The dependency on the quality and cost of GPT-40, lack of detailed prompt engineering analysis, and limited comparison with recent LLM-based CFE works are areas for improvement.

Given these considerations, the paper's novelty and significance are notable but not groundbreaking. I'd place the score at the higher end because of the high-plausibility and high-validity results.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Enhancing Test-Time Scaling of Large Language Models with Hierarchical Retrieval-Augmented MCTS
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05557v1)
- **Authors**: Alex ZH Dou, Zhongwei Wan, Dongfei Cui, Xin Wang, Jing Xiong, Haokun Lin, Chaofan Tao, Shen Yan, Mi Zhang
- **Abstract**: Test-time scaling has emerged as a promising paradigm in language modeling, leveraging additional computational resources at inference time to enhance model performance. In this work, we introduce R2-LLMs, a novel and versatile hierarchical retrieval-augmented reasoning framework designed to improve test-time scaling in large language models (LLMs) without requiring distillation from more advanced models to obtain chain-of-thought (CoT) training data. R2-LLMs enhances inference-time generalization by integrating dual-level retrieval-based in-context learning: (1) At the coarse level, our approach extracts abstract templates from complex reasoning problems and retrieves similar problem-answer pairs to facilitate high-level in-context learning; (2) At the fine level, during Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS), R2-LLMs efficiently retrieves analogous intermediate solution steps from reference mathematical problem datasets, refining step-wise reasoning with the aid of a process reward model (PRM) for scoring. R2-LLMs is a robust hierarchical reasoning-augmentation method that enhances in-context-level reasoning while seamlessly integrating with step-level tree search methods. Utilizing PRM, it refines both candidate generation and decision-making for improved reasoning accuracy. Empirical evaluations on the MATH500, GSM8K, and OlympiadBench-TO datasets achieve substantial relative improvement with an increase of up to 16% using LLaMA-3.1-8B compared to the baselines, showcasing the effectiveness of our approach in complex reasoning tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces R2-LLMs, a novel hierarchical retrieval-augmented reasoning framework designed to improve the performance of Large Language Models (LLMs) at inference time (test-time scaling). The approach utilizes a dual-level retrieval system: 1) a coarse-level retrieval of abstract problem templates and similar problem-answer pairs for high-level in-context learning, and 2) a fine-level retrieval during Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS) of analogous intermediate solution steps from reference mathematical datasets. A process reward model (PRM) is used to evaluate and score these steps. The method aims to enhance in-context learning while integrating seamlessly with tree search methods, refining candidate generation and decision-making. Experiments on MATH500, GSM8K, and OlympiadBench-TO datasets demonstrate improved performance compared to baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's core contribution lies in the *hierarchical* combination of retrieval mechanisms, integrating coarse-level template retrieval with fine-grained step retrieval within an MCTS framework. While retrieval-augmented methods and MCTS have been individually explored, the synergistic combination is relatively novel.The fine-grained step retrieval, coupled with the PRM scoring, has some overlap with existing approaches, but its specific integration with the coarser retrieval and MCTS framework gives it a reasonable degree of novelty.

*   **Significance:**  The paper addresses a crucial challenge in LLMs: enhancing reasoning capabilities at inference time without extensive retraining or distillation. Test-time scaling is a growing area, and R2-LLMs offers a promising approach to improve the effectiveness of search-based methods. The results demonstrate substantial improvements on challenging mathematical reasoning benchmarks, suggesting that R2-LLMs can generalize well and provide benefits in complex problem-solving.The potential for broad applicability is high, especially given the increasing focus on efficient inference techniques for LLMs. The ability to incorporate external knowledge effectively and refine intermediate reasoning steps significantly contributes to the field.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The hierarchical retrieval approach is well-motivated and addresses the limitations of relying solely on pre-trained knowledge or immediate problem context.
    *   The integration of coarse- and fine-grained retrieval with MCTS and a PRM is technically sound and well-explained.
    *   The empirical results are convincing, showing significant improvements over baselines on established benchmarks.
    *   The ablation studies and sensitivity analyses provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component and the impact of parameter choices.
    *  The code availability further enhances reproducibility and facilitates future research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The evaluation is primarily focused on mathematical reasoning tasks. More diverse benchmarks would strengthen the claim of general applicability.
    *   The paper acknowledges limitations in the types of models tested. It would be valuable to demonstrate effectiveness on larger, potentially closed-source, models.
    *   While the computational overhead analysis is included, a more detailed discussion of the trade-offs between performance gains and computational cost would be beneficial.
    *  There is some reliance on the DeepSeek and Mistral models for retrieval/PRM. It would be useful to test the sensitivity to these choices as well, and try to explore open-source alternatives to a greater extent.
    *   Some claims should be further analyzed with statistical significance testing to better validate the results.

*   **Potential Influence:** R2-LLMs has the potential to influence the field by promoting the use of hierarchical retrieval-augmentation strategies for test-time scaling. It offers a concrete and effective approach that can be adopted and extended by other researchers. It has the potential to inspire further research into more efficient and generalizable inference techniques for LLMs.

**Justification:**

The paper presents a reasonably novel and significant contribution to the field of language modeling and test-time scaling. The hierarchical retrieval approach and its integration with MCTS are well-motivated and implemented. The experimental results and analyses are solid. However, the limited evaluation domain and lack of testing on larger or closed-source models restricts its impact somewhat. It builds on previous work in retrieval augmentation and tree search methods, but the way these techniques are used in this study is novel, well-motivated, and well-executed. The results on public benchmarks certainly show promise, but more data will be needed to prove that these insights can translate to better LLM reasoning techniques more generally.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Search-based Selection of Metamorphic Relations for Optimized Robustness Testing of Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05565v1)
- **Authors**: Sangwon Hyun, Shaukat Ali, M. Ali Babar
- **Abstract**: Assessing the trustworthiness of Large Language Models (LLMs), such as robustness, has garnered significant attention. Recently, metamorphic testing that defines Metamorphic Relations (MRs) has been widely applied to evaluate the robustness of LLM executions. However, the MR-based robustness testing still requires a scalable number of MRs, thereby necessitating the optimization of selecting MRs. Most extant LLM testing studies are limited to automatically generating test cases (i.e., MRs) to enhance failure detection. Additionally, most studies only considered a limited test space of single perturbation MRs in their evaluation of LLMs. In contrast, our paper proposes a search-based approach for optimizing the MR groups to maximize failure detection and minimize the LLM execution cost. Moreover, our approach covers the combinatorial perturbations in MRs, facilitating the expansion of test space in the robustness assessment. We have developed a search process and implemented four search algorithms: Single-GA, NSGA-II, SPEA2, and MOEA/D with novel encoding to solve the MR selection problem in the LLM robustness testing. We conducted comparative experiments on the four search algorithms along with a random search, using two major LLMs with primary Text-to-Text tasks. Our statistical and empirical investigation revealed two key findings: (1) the MOEA/D algorithm performed the best in optimizing the MR space for LLM robustness testing, and (2) we identified silver bullet MRs for the LLM robustness testing, which demonstrated dominant capabilities in confusing LLMs across different Text-to-Text tasks. In LLM robustness assessment, our research sheds light on the fundamental problem for optimized testing and provides insights into search-based solutions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a search-based approach to optimize the selection of metamorphic relations (MRs) for robustness testing of Large Language Models (LLMs).  It aims to maximize failure detection and minimize LLM execution costs. The key idea is to use search algorithms to identify optimal subsets of MRs, considering combinatorial perturbations (multiple perturbations applied together). The authors implement and compare four search algorithms (Single-GA, NSGA-II, SPEA2, and MOEA/D) against a random search baseline on two text-to-text tasks using Gemini 1.5 Pro and Llama 3.1 70B models.  The results indicate that MOEA/D generally performs best in optimizing the MR space, while also identifying "silver bullet" MRs that are consistently effective across different LLMs and tasks. The study introduces novel encodings for MR-based test spaces.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents several novel contributions.  The application of search-based optimization to MR *selection* for LLM *robustness testing* is a significant contribution. Existing studies focus more on *generating* MRs automatically or applying them directly, not strategically *selecting* them.  The explicit consideration of *combinatorial* perturbations is also a valuable addition as it allows for a wider test space. The MR encoding designed for the search algorithms is novel and vital for the overall success of the approach.

*   **Significance:** Robustness testing of LLMs is critical for their safe and reliable deployment. Optimizing MR selection directly addresses the scalability issue of MR-based testing, making it more practical for real-world use. Identifying 'silver bullet' MRs, if validated further, could offer a practical shortcut for quickly assessing LLM robustness in similar text-to-text tasks. The comparison of different search algorithms provides valuable insights into which techniques are most effective for this specific problem.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of MR selection for LLM robustness testing and formulates it as an optimization problem.
    *   **Comprehensive Approach:**  It covers a wider test space with combinatorial perturbations and compares multiple search algorithms.
    *   **Empirical Evaluation:** The experiments are well-designed and use two prominent LLMs and representative tasks. The statistical analysis is rigorous.
    *   **Practical Insights:** Identification of silver bullet MRs and the analysis of algorithm execution overhead provide valuable practical guidance.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The authors release the data and code, enabling reproducibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Task Types:** The evaluation is limited to two text-to-text tasks (SA and TS).  It's unclear if the findings generalize to other LLM task types (e.g., question answering, code generation, image generation).
    *   **LLM Scope:** While using Gemini 1.5 pro and Llama3 is appropriate at time of publication, the LLM landscape is quickly evolving. Future work should extend the experiments to more LLMs, focusing on those with varying architectures and training data. The reliance on two particular LLMs also somewhat limits generalizability. The evaluation may be susceptible to architecture bias, where the method is most suited to a particular model topology, or data bias, where it is tuned implicitly to the training data of the models used.
    *   **Reliance on Existing MRs:** The approach relies on a predefined set of MRs (from METAL). The effectiveness of the overall method is therefore contingent on the quality and coverage of this initial MR set.  The process for creating new MRs should be considered as future direction, to increase generalizability.
    *   **Limited "Silver Bullet" Analysis:** While the paper identifies silver bullet MRs, the underlying *reasons* for their effectiveness are not explored in depth. A deeper analysis could lead to more generalizable principles for designing effective MRs.
    *   **Limited Search Algorithm Diversity:**  The search algorithms are all evolutionary approaches.  Considering other optimization techniques (e.g., reinforcement learning, Bayesian optimization) could potentially yield better results.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by:
    *   Encouraging more research into optimized MR selection for LLM testing.
    *   Providing a benchmark for comparing different MR selection techniques.
    *   Guiding practitioners in selecting effective MRs for specific LLM tasks.

**Score:** 7.5

**Justification:**

The paper makes a significant and novel contribution by addressing the important problem of MR selection for LLM robustness testing. The approach is well-defined, empirically evaluated, and provides practical insights. The main limitations are the limited scope of the evaluation (task types, LLMs) and a lack of deeper analysis into the reasons for the observed results. However, the strengths outweigh the weaknesses, making this a solid contribution to the field with the potential to influence future research and practice. A score of 7.5 reflects the significant novelty and importance tempered by the scope limitations.

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### SingLoRA: Low Rank Adaptation Using a Single Matrix
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05566v1)
- **Authors**: David Bensaïd, Noam Rotstein, Roy Velich, Daniel Bensaïd, Ron Kimmel
- **Abstract**: Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) has significantly advanced parameter-efficient fine-tuning of large pretrained models. LoRA augments the pre-trained weights of a model by adding the product of two smaller matrices that together form a low-rank matrix update. Recent research has shown that scale disparities between these two matrices often cause unstable training dynamics, leading to suboptimal performance. In this paper, we propose SingLoRA, which reformulates low-rank adaptation by learning the weights update as a decomposition of a single low-rank matrix multiplied by its transpose. This simple design inherently removes inter-matrix scale conflicts, ensuring stable optimization, and roughly halves the parameter count. We analyze SingLoRA within the infinite-width neural network framework, showing that it guarantees stable feature learning by construction. Extensive experiments on multiple tasks validate these benefits. In common sense reasoning, fine-tuning LLama 7B on MNLI with SingLoRA achieves 91.3% accuracy - surpassing LoRA (89.1%) and LoRA+ (90.2%) - while using only 60% of their parameter budget. In image generation, fine-tuning Stable Diffusion with SingLoRA significantly improves image fidelity on DreamBooth, achieving a DINO similarity score of 0.151, compared to scores of 0.148 and 0.143 for DoRA and LoRA, respectively.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the SingLoRA paper based on the provided text.

**Summary**

The paper introduces SingLoRA, a reformulation of Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) for parameter-efficient fine-tuning of large pre-trained models.  Instead of updating weights with the product of two low-rank matrices (as in LoRA), SingLoRA uses a single low-rank matrix multiplied by its transpose, resulting in a symmetric update.  The authors argue that this design inherently removes inter-matrix scale conflicts, promotes stable optimization, reduces the number of trainable parameters, and often improves performance. They provide theoretical analysis, showing SingLoRA guarantees stable feature learning in the infinite-width neural network setting. They then validate their method empirically on various tasks, including natural language understanding (GLUE benchmark) and image generation (DreamBooth). The results suggest that SingLoRA achieves better performance than LoRA and other variants (LoRA+, DoRA) while using fewer parameters and exhibiting more stable training dynamics.

**Critical Evaluation**

* **Novelty:** The primary novelty of this paper lies in the reformulation of LoRA using a single matrix multiplied by its transpose, leading to a symmetric update. The connection to scale imbalances in LoRA and the theoretical justification based on infinite-width networks are also notable. However, symmetric updates in related contexts aren't entirely new; this needs considering when gauging the overall novelty. This aspect significantly distinguishes it from prior work. The use of the transpose as a means to guarantee scale invariance is simple, elegant, and thus commendable.

* **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from its potential to address a key challenge in parameter-efficient fine-tuning – the unstable training dynamics often associated with LoRA.  If SingLoRA indeed provides a more stable and efficient alternative to LoRA, it could have a substantial impact on the adoption and performance of PEFT techniques. The empirical results presented are encouraging, showing improved performance on several benchmarks while reducing the parameter count. If the method can extend to larger and more varied setups than the ones presented, this impact would become greater.

* **Strengths:**
    *   **Theoretical Justification:** The analysis within the infinite-width neural network framework provides a strong theoretical basis for SingLoRA's stability claims.
    *   **Simplicity:** The reformulation is simple and easy to implement.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The paper presents a reasonable number of experiments across different modalities (NLP and image generation), showing consistent improvements over baselines.
    *   **Parameter Efficiency:** SingLoRA reduces the number of trainable parameters compared to LoRA, which is a desirable property for PEFT methods.
    *   **Addresses a Known Issue:** It tackles a well-documented problem of unstable training in LoRA caused by scale differences between adaptation matrices, a critical contribution.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope of Theoretical Analysis:** The theoretical analysis is based on the infinite-width network assumption, which might not perfectly reflect the behavior of real-world, finite-width models. This is a common limitation, but important to acknowledge.
    *   **Empirical Evaluation Limited to Certain Datasets and Models:**  The empirical validation could be broadened to include more diverse datasets, tasks, and model architectures. While the included experiments show improvement, strong claims require more comprehensive evidence. More experiments across diverse scales of models are needed to ensure it's robust.
    *   **Ramp Function Detail:** While the analysis studies the influence of T, more justification could be given on *why* a linear ramp is used versus other forms of ramp-up.

* **Potential Influence:** SingLoRA has the potential to influence the field by providing a more stable and efficient PEFT technique, which could lead to wider adoption and improved performance on downstream tasks. Also, others could explore ways of extending this architecture with other low-rank approaches, such as with DoRA's weight decomposition.

* **Justification:** The paper presents a clear contribution with solid theoretical justification and empirical evidence, which warrants a rating on the higher end. While the method seems simple, the results prove its benefits. However, the limitations in empirical scope and the assumption of the infinite-width setup prevent it from scoring much higher. The experiments show that the advantages in stability are present in practice. Therefore, more analysis would need to be done to further prove the advantages of the algorithm.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** SingLoRA represents a good contribution to parameter-efficient fine-tuning. It provides a novel and theoretically-justified approach for addressing the stability issues in LoRA, backed by promising empirical results. However, it's not groundbreaking enough to warrant a higher score, as the empirical results need to be further validated on a larger scale. Also, the infinite-width analysis is limited. However, the advantages in runtime make it more approachable in practice, as less time is needed for parameter tuning.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### ReLayout: Integrating Relation Reasoning for Content-aware Layout Generation with Multi-modal Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05568v1)
- **Authors**: Jiaxu Tian, Xuehui Yu, Yaoxing Wang, Pan Wang, Guangqian Guo, Shan Gao
- **Abstract**: Content-aware layout aims to arrange design elements appropriately on a given canvas to convey information effectively. Recently, the trend for this task has been to leverage large language models (LLMs) to generate layouts automatically, achieving remarkable performance. However, existing LLM-based methods fail to adequately interpret spatial relationships among visual themes and design elements, leading to structural and diverse problems in layout generation. To address this issue, we introduce ReLayout, a novel method that leverages relation-CoT to generate more reasonable and aesthetically coherent layouts by fundamentally originating from design concepts. Specifically, we enhance layout annotations by introducing explicit relation definitions, such as region, salient, and margin between elements, with the goal of decomposing the layout into smaller, structured, and recursive layouts, thereby enabling the generation of more structured layouts. Furthermore, based on these defined relationships, we introduce a layout prototype rebalance sampler, which defines layout prototype features across three dimensions and quantifies distinct layout styles. This sampler addresses uniformity issues in generation that arise from data bias in the prototype distribution balance process. Extensive experimental results verify that ReLayout outperforms baselines and can generate structural and diverse layouts that are more aligned with human aesthetics and more explainable.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper "ReLayout: Integrating Relation Reasoning for Content-aware Layout Generation with Multi-modal Large Language Models" introduces a new method, ReLayout, for content-aware layout generation. It aims to improve upon existing LLM-based approaches that often struggle with structural and diversity issues in generated layouts. ReLayout incorporates relation reasoning by explicitly modeling spatial relationships between design elements (region, saliency, margin) using a Chain-of-Thought (CoT) mechanism. It also introduces a layout prototype rebalance sampler to address data bias in the prototype distribution, promoting more diverse layouts. The paper includes a detailed description of the method, experimental results demonstrating improved performance over baselines, and user studies to validate the visual quality and diversity of the generated layouts. The paper also presents two datasets with enhanced layout information, which they publicly release.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The paper offers a notable advance over existing LLM-based layout generation methods by explicitly modeling element relationships. Previous methods mostly focused on element-level positioning based on the LLM's understanding. The introduction of relation-CoT is a novel way to infuse design logic into the layout generation process, making the output more structured and coherent. The layout prototype rebalance sampler is also a valuable contribution, as it tackles the data bias problem that can limit the diversity of generated layouts. The combination of relation reasoning and balanced sampling represents a significant improvement.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical gap in existing layout generation methods, which is the lack of structural organization and diversity. By explicitly modeling spatial relationships and balancing the layout prototypes, ReLayout is able to produce layouts that are more visually appealing and aligned with human aesthetic preferences. The release of the ReLayout datasets with enhanced layout information is also a valuable contribution, as it can facilitate future research in this area. The strong experimental results and user studies further validate the significance of the proposed method.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper provides a clear and well-structured explanation of the proposed method.
    *   The introduction of relation-CoT and layout prototype rebalance sampler is a novel approach to address the limitations of existing methods.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate significant improvements over baseline methods on both quantitative and qualitative metrics.
    *   The user studies provide strong evidence that ReLayout generates layouts that are more visually appealing and diverse.
    *   The release of the ReLayout datasets contributes to the advancement of the field.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the paper demonstrates improvements over existing methods, the generated layouts still exhibit some imperfections and may not always be fully optimized for specific design contexts.
    *   The method is computationally intensive and requires significant resources for training and inference. This could limit its applicability in resource-constrained environments.
    *   The paper mainly focuses on e-commerce posters, and the generalizability of ReLayout to other layout generation tasks (e.g., UI design, document layout) is not fully explored.
    *   The choice of fixed clustering K=8 in layout prototype rebalance sampler might limit layout generation in some cases.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to have a significant influence on the field of layout generation. The proposed method provides a promising direction for future research, and the ReLayout datasets can serve as a valuable resource for training and evaluating new models. The paper could inspire researchers to explore other ways of incorporating design knowledge and reasoning into layout generation systems.

*   **Rigorous Evaluation:** The paper offers a thorough comparison of the performance of ReLayout and multiple recent SOTA methods with quantitative evaluation and qualitative visualisations. The user study is comprehensive and proves that ReLayout indeed better performs at aligning to human design principles.

*   **Scope for Improvement:** To further improve this work and the impact of this paper, future steps can include but not limited to:
    *  Exploring more diverse regions (e.g., adding salient objects in the empty regions), and more complex relation structures to build more powerful content aware relations.
    *  Reducing model sizes so it can be deployed to more cases.
    *  More generalisation studies on how well ReLayout performs on different tasks.

**Score: 8.5**

**Justification:** The paper makes a substantial contribution to content-aware layout generation by introducing novel techniques for modeling element relationships and balancing layout prototypes. It addresses a clear gap in existing LLM-based methods and demonstrates strong performance improvements. While there are some limitations in terms of computational cost, generalizability, and scope for future improvements, the overall quality of the work and its potential impact on the field justify a high score. The paper is well-written, thoroughly evaluated, and offers valuable insights into the challenges and opportunities in layout generation. The rigorousness in the experiments and the release of the dataset solidify the high score and justify why it is a highly noteworthy paper that will likely inspire new approaches in generative LLM-based layout design.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Prompt Migration: Stabilizing GenAI Applications with Evolving Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05573v1)
- **Authors**: Shivani Tripathi, Pushpanjali Nema, Aditya Halder, Shi Qiao, Alekh Jindal
- **Abstract**: Generative AI is transforming business applications by enabling natural language interfaces and intelligent automation. However, the underlying large language models (LLMs) are evolving rapidly and so prompting them consistently is a challenge. This leads to inconsistent and unpredictable application behavior, undermining the reliability that businesses require for mission-critical workflows. In this paper, we introduce the concept of prompt migration as a systematic approach to stabilizing GenAI applications amid changing LLMs. Using the Tursio enterprise search application as a case study, we analyze the impact of successive GPT model upgrades, detail our migration framework including prompt redesign and a migration testbed, and demonstrate how these techniques restore application consistency. Our results show that structured prompt migration can fully recover the application reliability that was lost due to model drift. We conclude with practical lessons learned, emphasizing the need for prompt lifecycle management and robust testing to ensure dependable GenAI-powered business applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces the concept of "prompt migration" as a method to stabilize GenAI applications that are built on rapidly evolving Large Language Models (LLMs). It describes a case study involving the Tursio enterprise search application, initially built on GPT-4-32k, which had to be migrated to newer GPT models due to deprecation. The paper analyzes the impact of these model upgrades, details a prompt migration framework (including prompt redesign and a migration testbed), and demonstrates how these techniques restored application consistency. The authors emphasize the importance of prompt lifecycle management and robust testing for ensuring the reliability of GenAI-powered business applications. They share practical lessons learned from their migration experience.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The concept of "prompt migration" is valuable. While prompt engineering is a well-established area, the idea of systematically managing prompts as LLMs evolve is relatively new. The paper successfully formalizes this need and proposes a structured approach.

**Significance:**

The significance of the paper lies in addressing a very practical problem: maintaining the stability and reliability of GenAI applications in the face of rapidly changing LLMs. The paper's focus on enterprise applications enhances its importance, given the stringent reliability requirements in these contexts. The case study of Tursio is useful for illustrating the challenges and solutions. The insights related to prompt lifecycle management and differences in model behaviors are valuable to practitioners.

**Strengths:**

*   **Practical Problem:** Addresses a real and pressing issue in the deployment of GenAI applications.
*   **Case Study:** Provides a concrete example with the Tursio application, making the concepts more relatable.
*   **Framework:** Offers a structured framework for prompt migration that can be adapted to other applications.
*   **Lessons Learned:** Shares practical insights and recommendations for managing prompts in a dynamic LLM environment.
*   **Discussion of Model Differences:** Acknowledges and analyzes the shifts in model behavior between different GPT versions.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Generalizability:** The case study is specific to an enterprise search application. While the framework is presented as adaptable, the specific techniques and prompt examples may not directly translate to other application domains.
*   **Lack of Empirical Validation:** While the paper reports test results, it would benefit from a more rigorous empirical evaluation of the prompt migration framework, comparing its performance against alternative approaches.
*   **Automated Prompt Migration:** The paper mentions automation as future work. The reliance on manual prompt updates is a limitation.
*   **Limited Scope of Migration Testbed** - The size and complexity of the migration testbed may not be sufficient to guarantee consistent model performance across all queries.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution by highlighting and addressing the prompt migration issue, offering a practical framework, and sharing insights based on real-world experience. However, there are a few limitations: the case study is very specific, more rigorous empirical validation and stronger automation would be needed to increase the paper's practical impact. Also, although it is clearly written, the formatting of the references is unconventional, which makes it seem hastily prepared.

### Beyond Retrieval: Ensembling Cross-Encoders and GPT Rerankers with LLMs for Biomedical QA
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05577v1)
- **Authors**: Shashank Verma, Fengyi Jiang, Xiangning Xue
- **Abstract**: Biomedical semantic question answering rooted in information retrieval can play a crucial role in keeping up to date with vast, rapidly evolving and ever-growing biomedical literature. A robust system can help researchers, healthcare professionals and even layman users access relevant knowledge grounded in evidence. The BioASQ 2025 Task13b Challenge serves as an important benchmark, offering a competitive platform for advancement of this space. This paper presents the methodologies and results from our participation in this challenge where we built a Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) system that can answer biomedical questions by retrieving relevant PubMed documents and snippets to generate answers. For the retrieval task, we generated dense embeddings from biomedical articles for initial retrieval, and applied an ensemble of finetuned cross-encoders and large language models (LLMs) for re-ranking to identify top relevant documents. Our solution achieved an MAP@10 of 0.1581, placing 10th on the leaderboard for the retrieval task. For answer generation, we employed few-shot prompting of instruction-tuned LLMs. Our system achieved macro-F1 score of 0.95 for yes/no questions (rank 12), Mean Reciprocal Rank (MRR) of 0.64 for factoid questions (rank 1), mean-F1 score of 0.63 for list questions (rank 5), and ROUGE-SU4 F1 score of 0.29 for ideal answers (rank 11).
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) system for biomedical question answering, developed for the BioASQ Task 13b challenge at CLEF 2025. The system focuses on both document retrieval and answer generation. For retrieval, the authors used a dense retrieval approach, indexing PubMed articles with `bge-large-en` embeddings. They employed a two-stage re-ranking strategy: an ensemble of fine-tuned cross-encoders and large language models (LLMs) (specifically GPT-40-turbo). For answer generation, they utilized few-shot prompting of instruction-tuned LLMs (GPT-40-turbo and Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3). The paper details various experimental setups, including different prompting strategies, cross-encoder finetuning variations, and ensembling techniques. The system achieved competitive results, ranking 10th in document retrieval (MAP@10 of 0.1581) and performing well across different question types in answer generation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Approach:** The paper provides a detailed account of the entire RAG pipeline, from dataset preparation to answer generation.  This thoroughness allows for reproducibility and provides insights for other researchers.
    *   **Effective Retrieval Strategy:** The use of `bge-large-en` for initial retrieval, combined with a two-stage re-ranking process (cross-encoders and LLMs), is a well-established technique, and the authors demonstrate its effectiveness in the biomedical domain. The ablation studies with various fine-tuning approaches on the cross-encoders provides useful insight.
    *   **Prompting Strategy Exploration:** The exploration of different prompting strategies for LLM-based answer generation is valuable. The paper provides specific details about the templates used and their impact on performance.
    *   **Competitive Results:** Achieving a top-10 ranking in the BioASQ challenge suggests the system's effectiveness and relevance. The detailed reporting of metrics for each question type adds to the credibility.
    *   **Reproducibility Considerations:**  The authors acknowledge the challenges encountered (memory limitations, unstable gradients) and explicitly provide intent to release code on GitHub, promoting reproducibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Novelty in Approach:** The overall architecture (dense retrieval, cross-encoder re-ranking, LLM generation) is a standard RAG framework. The novelty primarily lies in the specific implementation choices (e.g., the particular models used, fine-tuning techniques) and their application to the biomedical domain. The use of BioASQ as an evaluation dataset provides some domain adaptation focus.
    *   **Incremental Improvements:** While the system achieves good results, the reported improvements from ensembling and GPT-based re-ranking seem relatively small.  This suggests that the majority of the performance gain is from the initial dense retrieval and cross-encoder re-ranking stages.
    *   **Lack of In-depth Error Analysis:**  The paper lacks a detailed qualitative analysis of the system's errors.  Understanding the types of questions the system struggles with and the reasons behind those failures would provide more valuable insights. The discussion about problems with overly long or repetitive snippets in yes/no question answering is useful, but more comprehensive analysis is needed.
    *   **Fine-tuning Results Omitted:** The disappointing results of BioGPT fine-tuning are mentioned, but details are omitted "due to less competitive results." While transparency is intended via open-sourcing the code, a brief explanation of why fine-tuning was unsuccessful would be beneficial.  It would save readers from repeating unsuccessful efforts.
    *   **Uneven Participation in Challenge Batches:** The lack of participation in all Phase A challenge batches prevents direct comparisons of the system's performance over time and relative to other participants.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Demonstrates Effectiveness of RAG for Biomedical QA:** The paper reinforces the suitability of the RAG framework for tackling biomedical question answering.
    *   **Provides Practical Insights:** The specific implementation details, including model choices, training strategies, and prompting techniques, offer practical guidance for researchers and practitioners working in this area.
    *   **Offers a Baseline for Future Work:** The system's performance on the BioASQ dataset can serve as a baseline for future research in biomedical question answering.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper is a solid engineering effort that effectively applies existing RAG techniques to the biomedical domain. While the overall approach lacks significant novelty, the thoroughness of the implementation, the detailed experimental analysis, and the competitive results warrant a decent score. The insights gained from the prompt engineering and the combination of cross-encoders and LLMs are valuable.  The limitations of the paper (lack of in-depth error analysis, omitted fine-tuning details) prevent it from achieving a higher score.

### The Landscape of Memorization in LLMs: Mechanisms, Measurement, and Mitigation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05578v1)
- **Authors**: Alexander Xiong, Xuandong Zhao, Aneesh Pappu, Dawn Song
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated remarkable capabilities across a wide range of tasks, yet they also exhibit memorization of their training data. This phenomenon raises critical questions about model behavior, privacy risks, and the boundary between learning and memorization. Addressing these concerns, this paper synthesizes recent studies and investigates the landscape of memorization, the factors influencing it, and methods for its detection and mitigation. We explore key drivers, including training data duplication, training dynamics, and fine-tuning procedures that influence data memorization. In addition, we examine methodologies such as prefix-based extraction, membership inference, and adversarial prompting, assessing their effectiveness in detecting and measuring memorized content. Beyond technical analysis, we also explore the broader implications of memorization, including the legal and ethical implications. Finally, we discuss mitigation strategies, including data cleaning, differential privacy, and post-training unlearning, while highlighting open challenges in balancing the minimization of harmful memorization with utility. This paper provides a comprehensive overview of the current state of research on LLM memorization across technical, privacy, and performance dimensions, identifying critical directions for future work.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "The Landscape of Memorization in LLMs: Mechanisms, Measurement, and Mitigation":

**Summary:**

The paper presents a comprehensive overview of the phenomenon of memorization in Large Language Models (LLMs). It synthesizes recent research, exploring the underlying mechanisms influencing memorization, methods for its detection and measurement, and strategies for mitigation. The paper delves into factors like training data duplication, training dynamics, and fine-tuning procedures, examining their impact on data memorization. It also evaluates various detection techniques, including prefix-based extraction, membership inference, and adversarial prompting, assessing their effectiveness. Finally, the paper discusses mitigation approaches such as data cleaning, differential privacy, and post-training unlearning, highlighting challenges in balancing utility with minimizing harmful memorization. The paper identifies critical future research directions related to this complex topic.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Scope:** The paper provides a well-structured and detailed review, covering a broad range of aspects related to LLM memorization. It effectively organizes information from diverse research areas, creating a cohesive understanding of the field.
    *   **Clear Taxonomy:** The visual taxonomy (Figure 1) is helpful in mapping and categorizing the complex landscape of memorization. This visual representation aids in understanding the relationships between different concepts.
    *   **Identifies Open Questions:** The paper effectively highlights several important open questions, stimulating further research and discussion within the community. These questions help to focus future efforts on addressing critical gaps in knowledge.
    *   **Bridging Technical and Ethical Aspects:** The paper goes beyond purely technical considerations, addressing the privacy, legal, and ethical implications of memorization, and thus provides a more complete picture of the problem.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Novelty in Insights:** While the paper synthesizes existing research effectively, it doesn't offer substantial new insights or breakthroughs. It primarily compiles and summarizes existing knowledge rather than generating new perspectives or findings.
    *   **Lacks Deep Dive in Mitigation Strategies:** Although the paper mentions various mitigation techniques, a more in-depth analysis of the pros and cons, and practical challenges of each strategy could have strengthened the paper.
    *   **Limited Comparative Analysis:** There could be further comparative analysis of the different detection methods mentioned. A head-to-head comparison and practical guidance around when to choose one method over another could improve the value of the paper.

*   **Significance and Impact:**
    *   The paper's value lies in providing a valuable resource for researchers and practitioners entering the field of LLM security and privacy. It serves as a good starting point for understanding the complexities of memorization and its implications.
    *   The paper can indirectly influence the field by highlighting critical research gaps and inspiring new investigations into mitigation strategies and more effective detection techniques.
    *   The paper makes the discussion about memorization, which is often very technical, accessible to a broader audience including those concerned with legal and ethical implications.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper serves as a valuable survey and synthesis of current knowledge in a rapidly evolving field. It offers a helpful overview, identifies key challenges, and provides a roadmap for future research. However, its lack of substantial novel insights and limited depth in certain areas (mitigation strategies, comparative analysis of detection methods) prevents it from achieving a higher score. Overall, it's a competent and useful review, but not a transformative contribution.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Model-free Optical Processors using In Situ Reinforcement Learning with Proximal Policy Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05583v1)
- **Authors**: Yuhang Li, Shiqi Chen, Tingyu Gong, Aydogan Ozcan
- **Abstract**: Optical computing holds promise for high-speed, energy-efficient information processing, with diffractive optical networks emerging as a flexible platform for implementing task-specific transformations.
