### LLMs are Introvert
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05638v1)
- **Authors**: Litian Zhang, Xiaoming Zhang, Bingyu Yan, Ziyi Zhou, Bo Zhang, Zhenyu Guan, Xi Zhang, Chaozhuo Li
- **Abstract**: The exponential growth of social media and generative AI has transformed information dissemination, fostering connectivity but also accelerating the spread of misinformation. Understanding information propagation dynamics and developing effective control strategies is essential to mitigate harmful content. Traditional models, such as SIR, provide basic insights but inadequately capture the complexities of online interactions. Advanced methods, including attention mechanisms and graph neural networks, enhance accuracy but typically overlook user psychology and behavioral dynamics. Large language models (LLMs), with their human-like reasoning, offer new potential for simulating psychological aspects of information spread. We introduce an LLM-based simulation environment capturing agents' evolving attitudes, emotions, and responses. Initial experiments, however, revealed significant gaps between LLM-generated behaviors and authentic human dynamics, especially in stance detection and psychological realism. A detailed evaluation through Social Information Processing Theory identified major discrepancies in goal-setting and feedback evaluation, stemming from the lack of emotional processing in standard LLM training. To address these issues, we propose the Social Information Processing-based Chain of Thought (SIP-CoT) mechanism enhanced by emotion-guided memory. This method improves the interpretation of social cues, personalization of goals, and evaluation of feedback. Experimental results confirm that SIP-CoT-enhanced LLM agents more effectively process social information, demonstrating behaviors, attitudes, and emotions closer to real human interactions. In summary, this research highlights critical limitations in current LLM-based propagation simulations and demonstrates how integrating SIP-CoT and emotional memory significantly enhances the social intelligence and realism of LLM agents.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper explores the limitations of Large Language Models (LLMs) in simulating human social behavior within agent-based simulations. The authors argue that current LLMs are "introverted" and lack the nuance necessary to accurately model social cognition, leading to unrealistic simulation results.  They evaluate LLMs using the Social Information Processing (SIP) theory and identify key deficiencies in cue encoding, interpretation, goal setting, and response evaluation. To address these shortcomings, they propose a Social Information Processing-based Chain of Thought (SIP-CoT) mechanism with emotion-guided memory, enhancing LLMs' ability to process social information and exhibit more human-like behaviors. They construct a dataset for social simulation and conduct experiments demonstrating that their SIP-CoT approach improves the social intelligence of LLM agents.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The idea of using LLMs for social simulations is not entirely new. However, the paper's novelty lies in its detailed, psychologically grounded evaluation of LLMs' social cognitive abilities and its attempt to address these limitations through the SIP-CoT mechanism.  The rigorous application of the SIP framework and the creation of a dedicated SIP-testing dataset represent a significant contribution. The design of SIP-CoT and emotion-guided memory also introduces a novel integration strategy, though the technical details of this mechanism could be more elaborate.
* **Significance:**  Accurate social simulations are crucial for understanding complex social dynamics and developing effective interventions. By identifying and addressing the limitations of LLMs in this domain, the paper has the potential to significantly improve the realism and reliability of agent-based simulations. This has implications for fields like social science, policy-making, and human-agent interaction. The work also makes progress towards the larger goal of making AI systems more socially intelligent.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Rigorous Evaluation:** The paper utilizes a well-established psychological framework (SIP theory) to evaluate LLMs, providing a structured and comprehensive analysis of their social cognitive abilities.
    * **Targeted Approach:**  The SIP-CoT mechanism directly addresses the identified limitations, showing a clear understanding of the problem and a focused solution.
    * **Comprehensive Experimentation:**  The authors present a thorough evaluation of their approach, using both macro-level (global propagation) and micro-level (agent alignment) metrics.
    * **Well-defined Dataset:** The creation of a dataset for social simulation based on Reddit data is valuable for the community.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Technical Depth:** While the SIP-CoT mechanism is conceptually sound, the paper could benefit from a more detailed explanation of its implementation. For example, it is unclear how emotion-guided memory is integrated and managed in practice.
    * **Limited Scope:**  The experiments are limited to text-based social interactions.  The approach needs to be tested with more diverse modalities (e.g., visual cues, speech) to assess its generalizability.
    * **Lack of Comparison to other Methods:** The paper doesn't seem to compare the proposed SIP-CoT approach to other techniques that enhance the social intelligence of agents, which makes it hard to establish its relative advantage.
    * **Modest Results:** While the paper reports improvements with SIP-CoT, the overall social dynamics is still far from accurately representing real-world data.

**Justification:**

This paper represents a valuable contribution to the field by identifying specific weaknesses of LLMs in simulating human social behavior and proposing a targeted solution. The application of SIP theory and the creation of a dedicated dataset are key strengths. However, the paper could be strengthened by providing more technical details, expanding the scope of the experiments, comparing the proposed methods to alternative approaches and providing more impressive results.

### Knowledge-guided Complex Diffusion Model for PolSAR Image Classification in Contourlet Domain
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05666v1)
- **Authors**: Junfei Shi, Yu Cheng, Haiyan Jin, Junhuai Li, Zhaolin Xiao, Maoguo Gong, Weisi Lin
- **Abstract**: Diffusion models have demonstrated exceptional performance across various domains due to their ability to model and generate complicated data distributions. However, when applied to PolSAR data, traditional real-valued diffusion models face challenges in capturing complex-valued phase information.Moreover, these models often struggle to preserve fine structural details. To address these limitations, we leverage the Contourlet transform, which provides rich multiscale and multidirectional representations well-suited for PolSAR imagery. We propose a structural knowledge-guided complex diffusion model for PolSAR image classification in the Contourlet domain. Specifically, the complex Contourlet transform is first applied to decompose the data into low- and high-frequency subbands, enabling the extraction of statistical and boundary features. A knowledge-guided complex diffusion network is then designed to model the statistical properties of the low-frequency components. During the process, structural information from high-frequency coefficients is utilized to guide the diffusion process, improving edge preservation. Furthermore, multiscale and multidirectional high-frequency features are jointly learned to further boost classification accuracy. Experimental results on three real-world PolSAR datasets demonstrate that our approach surpasses state-of-the-art methods, particularly in preserving edge details and maintaining region homogeneity in complex terrain.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a "Knowledge-guided Complex Diffusion Model" (CD-KCDM) for PolSAR image classification. It addresses limitations of standard diffusion models when applied to PolSAR data, specifically their struggles with complex-valued phase information and preserving fine structural details. The key innovation is leveraging the complex Contourlet transform to decompose PolSAR images into low-frequency (statistical texture) and high-frequency (structural edges) subbands. A knowledge-guided complex diffusion network models the low-frequency components, using structural information from the high-frequency coefficients to guide the diffusion process and improve edge preservation. A cross-attention mechanism further enhances high-frequency features, which are then fused with the learned low-frequency representation for classification. Experiments on three PolSAR datasets demonstrate improved performance compared to state-of-the-art methods, particularly in edge detail preservation and region homogeneity.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Addressing a relevant problem:** The paper tackles a genuine challenge in applying diffusion models to PolSAR data. PolSAR data's complex-valued nature and the importance of structural information (edges) make it a non-trivial application domain for standard diffusion techniques.
*   **Effective Integration of Contourlet Transform:**  The clever use of the Contourlet transform is a major strength. Decomposing the data and using the high-frequency components as structural "knowledge" to guide the low-frequency diffusion is a well-reasoned approach. This allows the model to learn both statistical and structural features effectively.
*   **Knowledge-Guided Diffusion:** Guiding the diffusion process with structural information is a novel and potentially impactful approach. This departs from purely data-driven diffusion models and incorporates prior knowledge to improve performance.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents a thorough evaluation on multiple real-world PolSAR datasets, comparing against several state-of-the-art methods. Ablation studies are used to justify the contribution of each module.
*   **Clear Methodology:** The paper describes the methodology clearly and provides detailed explanation on the modules and training process.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Novelty:**  While the combination of Contourlet transform and diffusion models guided by structural knowledge is novel, the individual components are well-established.  The core diffusion model architecture itself doesn't appear to be substantially modified beyond handling complex values, so some aspects of the work are based on existing techniques.
*   **Complexity:** The proposed architecture is relatively complex with multiple modules (CD-KCDM, SKEM, CAFE). It is more difficult to reproduce and understand than other methods. This could hinder its widespread adoption and further improvement by other researchers.
*   **Limited Generality:** The strong reliance on the Contourlet transform might limit the method's applicability to other types of remote sensing data or image modalities where such decompositions are not readily available or as effective.
*   **Lack of visual results and discussion**: More visual comparisons would improve the analysis of the results. Discussion on parameter tuning process would benefit future readers.

**Significance:**

The paper's significance lies in: (1) Demonstrating that structural knowledge can effectively guide diffusion models for improved performance in PolSAR image classification, (2) Highlighting the suitability of Contourlet transforms for PolSAR data analysis, particularly in separating statistical texture and structural edge information, and (3) Improving classification accuracy and enhancing edge preservation compared to existing methods, which is crucial for practical applications of PolSAR imagery. The proposed knowledge-guided framework could inspire new approaches for applying diffusion models in other domains where domain-specific prior knowledge can be leveraged.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper offers a useful advancement in the application of diffusion models to a specific remote sensing problem.  While the individual components are not radically new, their integration is clever and results in improved performance. The novelty of incorporating structural knowledge via high-frequency Contourlet coefficients into the diffusion process is valuable. However, the reliance on Contourlet transform limits the generality of the model. Considering the limitations, a score of 7 reflects the paper's contribution.

### Modeling and Reversing Brain Lesions Using Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05670v1)
- **Authors**: Omar Zamzam, Haleh Akrami, Anand Joshi, Richard Leahy
- **Abstract**: Brain lesions are abnormalities or injuries in brain tissue that are often detectable using magnetic resonance imaging (MRI), which reveals structural changes in the affected areas. This broad definition of brain lesions includes areas of the brain that are irreversibly damaged, as well as areas of brain tissue that are deformed as a result of lesion growth or swelling. Despite the importance of differentiating between damaged and deformed tissue, existing lesion segmentation methods overlook this distinction, labeling both of them as a single anomaly. In this work, we introduce a diffusion model-based framework for analyzing and reversing the brain lesion process. Our pipeline first segments abnormal regions in the brain, then estimates and reverses tissue deformations by restoring displaced tissue to its original position, isolating the core lesion area representing the initial damage. Finally, we inpaint the core lesion area to arrive at an estimation of the pre-lesion healthy brain. This proposed framework reverses a forward lesion growth process model that is well-established in biomechanical studies that model brain lesions. Our results demonstrate improved accuracy in lesion segmentation, characterization, and brain labeling compared to traditional methods, offering a robust tool for clinical and research applications in brain lesion analysis. Since pre-lesion healthy versions of abnormal brains are not available in any public dataset for validation of the reverse process, we simulate a forward model to synthesize multiple lesioned brain images.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a diffusion model-based framework for analyzing and "reversing" the brain lesion process. The method involves: 1) Segmenting abnormal brain regions in an MRI scan. 2) Estimating and reversing tissue deformations to restore displaced tissue and isolate the core lesion area (representing irreversible damage). 3) Inpainting the core lesion area to estimate the pre-lesion healthy brain. To validate the "reversal" process, the authors simulate a forward lesion growth process using a biomechanical model and synthesize lesioned brain images, as pre-lesion healthy images are unavailable. The results demonstrate improved accuracy in lesion segmentation, characterization, and brain labeling compared to traditional methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The approach of "reversing" the lesion process using diffusion models is somewhat novel. Traditional methods primarily focus on segmentation and classification. The idea of reconstructing the pre-lesion state, even if only an estimate, is an interesting one. However, the individual components – diffusion-based inpainting, registration, and segmentation – are not individually groundbreaking. The novelty resides more in their combination and application to this specific problem.

*   **Significance:** The potential clinical significance hinges on the accuracy and reliability of the pre-lesion reconstruction. If successful, it could improve surgical planning by differentiating between damaged and deformed tissue, and it could aid in monitoring post-operative recovery. Improving brain labeling accuracy in the presence of lesions is also a useful outcome. The synthesis method is also valuable because it allows generating labeled lesioned data, which can be scarce.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The integration of a forward biomechanical model and a reverse diffusion model provides a coherent framework.
    *   The paper demonstrates quantitative improvements in lesion segmentation (DICE score).
    *   The authors address a significant problem in medical imaging: the difficulty of analyzing brains with structural abnormalities.
    *   The simulated lesion data generation enables controlled experiments and validation.
    *   Provides a method for enhancing the accuracy of established brain labelling software.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The validation of the "reversal" process relies heavily on *simulated* lesions. While the biomechanical model is well-established, it's a simplification of the complex biological processes involved in lesion development. The accuracy of the pre-lesion estimation on *real* lesions is not directly demonstrated. The "ground truth" only exists in the simulated data.
    *   The quantitative evaluation of the inpainting module is somewhat limited, relying on SSIM and NMSE. While these are common metrics, they don't fully capture the anatomical plausibility of the generated tissue.
    *   The performance improvement in brain labeling, while shown, might be marginal in certain clinical scenarios. The mislabelling corrected in Figure 8 does not seem like a high impact issue.

*   **Impact:** The paper's impact depends on how well the proposed methods generalize to real-world scenarios with diverse lesion types. The synthesis method of lesioned brain imaging can also impact by enriching data for training learning algorithms. Also, the proposed "reversal" process can be more accurate by enhancing each of its components (lesion segmentation and inpainting). The study has value in advancing research for analyzing brains with lesions by providing a robust tool for future evaluation.

*   **Justification for Score:** The paper presents a novel approach to a relevant problem, but the validation is primarily on simulated data, and the improvements in inpainting and brain labeling are incremental. While the combination of methods is compelling, the individual contributions of each technique are not significant. The work serves a robust tool for the analysis of brains with lesions.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Integrating Diffusion-based Multi-task Learning with Online Reinforcement Learning for Robust Quadruped Robot Control
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05674v1)
- **Authors**: Xinyao Qin, Xiaoteng Ma, Yang Qi, Qihan Liu, Chuanyi Xue, Ning Gui, Qinyu Dong, Jun Yang, Bin Liang
- **Abstract**: Recent research has highlighted the powerful capabilities of imitation learning in robotics. Leveraging generative models, particularly diffusion models, these approaches offer notable advantages such as strong multi-task generalization, effective language conditioning, and high sample efficiency. While their application has been successful in manipulation tasks, their use in legged locomotion remains relatively underexplored, mainly due to compounding errors that affect stability and difficulties in task transition under limited data. Online reinforcement learning (RL) has demonstrated promising results in legged robot control in the past years, providing valuable insights to address these challenges. In this work, we propose DMLoco, a diffusion-based framework for quadruped robots that integrates multi-task pretraining with online PPO finetuning to enable language-conditioned control and robust task transitions. Our approach first pretrains the policy on a diverse multi-task dataset using diffusion models, enabling language-guided execution of various skills. Then, it finetunes the policy in simulation to ensure robustness and stable task transition during real-world deployment. By utilizing Denoising Diffusion Implicit Models (DDIM) for efficient sampling and TensorRT for optimized deployment, our policy runs onboard at 50Hz, offering a scalable and efficient solution for adaptive, language-guided locomotion on resource-constrained robotic platforms.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DMLoco, a novel framework for controlling quadruped robots. It integrates diffusion-based multi-task pretraining with online Proximal Policy Optimization (PPO) finetuning. The initial pretraining phase uses a diffusion model to learn a language-conditioned policy from a diverse set of locomotion skills. Subsequent PPO finetuning in simulation enhances robustness and enables stable gait transitions. The framework leverages Denoising Diffusion Implicit Models (DDIM) for efficient sampling and TensorRT for optimized onboard deployment, achieving real-time inference on resource-constrained platforms. The core contributions are: a new multi-task quadruped learning framework; stable task transitions without explicit gait transition data; a language-conditioned control interface; and successful real-world deployment.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The integration of diffusion models with online reinforcement learning for quadruped locomotion is a notable combination. While each component (diffusion models, RL, language conditioning) has been explored separately, their synergistic application in a real-world robotics context, specifically for legged locomotion, provides a worthwhile contribution. The idea of using imitation learning for pre-training from a wide range of skills and then fine-tuning using online RL has also been explored, but here, that idea is adapted well for diffusion models and applied to a more complicated robotic task.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses several key challenges in quadruped robot control: generalization to diverse tasks, stability during transitions, and real-time performance on limited hardware. The language conditioning aspect improves human-robot interaction. The success in achieving real-time control at 50Hz onboard a Jetson Orin NX is significant for practical deployment. Moreover, the observed smaller "sim-to-real" gap following fine-tuning suggests the method's robustness. There are a few prior works, such as DiffuseLoco [26], that use diffusion models for legged locomotion, but DMLoco differentiates itself by using diffusion models for offline imitation learning and then performing an online PPO fine-tuning.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges of applying diffusion models to legged locomotion.
    *   **Well-Defined Approach:** The DMLoco framework is well-explained, with a logical progression from pretraining to finetuning.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of DMLoco in simulation and real-world scenarios. The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of individual components.
    *   **Real-World Deployment:** The successful deployment on a physical robot and the achievement of real-time control are compelling evidence of the method's practicality.
    *   **Language Conditioning and Generalization:** The demonstration of language-conditioned control and promising generalization to unseen natural language commands adds value.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Baseline Comparison:** Although the baselines (IBC, BeT, DiffuseLoco) are relevant, more direct comparisons to state-of-the-art online reinforcement learning approaches for legged locomotion that incorporate imitation learning techniques would be beneficial.
    *   **Reward Engineering:** While the paper simplifies the reward function during finetuning, details on the precise reward formulation are somewhat lacking.
    *   **Limited Task Diversity:** The quadruped is only taught four locomotion skills, this could be expanded.

*   **Potential Influence:** The DMLoco framework has the potential to influence future research in legged robot control by providing a practical and effective approach for combining generative models with online reinforcement learning. The insights into the role of language conditioning and real-time deployment are also valuable for the robotics community.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, I would assign a score of **7**.

The paper provides a well-executed and practical solution to challenges in quadruped locomotion. The integration of diffusion models and online RL is a novel and promising approach. However, the weaknesses mentioned above prevent it from achieving a higher score. Specifically, the task diversity should be increased to demonstrate a stronger ability for generalization, and more comparisons should be done between DMLoco to other current state-of-the-art approaches. Also, while language conditioning improves human-robot interaction, this is not emphasized as much within the paper.

### LiON-LoRA: Rethinking LoRA Fusion to Unify Controllable Spatial and Temporal Generation for Video Diffusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05678v1)
- **Authors**: Yisu Zhang, Chenjie Cao, Chaohui Yu, Jianke Zhu
- **Abstract**: Video Diffusion Models (VDMs) have demonstrated remarkable capabilities in synthesizing realistic videos by learning from large-scale data. Although vanilla Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) can learn specific spatial or temporal movement to driven VDMs with constrained data, achieving precise control over both camera trajectories and object motion remains challenging due to the unstable fusion and non-linear scalability. To address these issues, we propose LiON-LoRA, a novel framework that rethinks LoRA fusion through three core principles: Linear scalability, Orthogonality, and Norm consistency. First, we analyze the orthogonality of LoRA features in shallow VDM layers, enabling decoupled low-level controllability. Second, norm consistency is enforced across layers to stabilize fusion during complex camera motion combinations. Third, a controllable token is integrated into the diffusion transformer (DiT) to linearly adjust motion amplitudes for both cameras and objects with a modified self-attention mechanism to ensure decoupled control. Additionally, we extend LiON-LoRA to temporal generation by leveraging static-camera videos, unifying spatial and temporal controllability. Experiments demonstrate that LiON-LoRA outperforms state-of-the-art methods in trajectory control accuracy and motion strength adjustment, achieving superior generalization with minimal training data. Project Page: https://fuchengsu.github.io/lionlora.github.io/
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the LION-LoRA paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "LION-LoRA: Rethinking LoRA Fusion to Unify Controllable Spatial and Temporal Generation for Video Diffusion" addresses the challenge of achieving precise and stable control over both camera trajectories and object motion in video diffusion models (VDMs) using Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA).  The authors propose LiON-LoRA, a framework built upon three core principles: Linear scalability, Orthogonality, and Norm consistency. It analyzes the orthogonality of LoRA features in shallow layers for decoupled low-level control, enforces norm consistency across layers for fusion stability, and integrates a controllable token for linear adjustment of motion amplitudes. It also extends LoRA into temporal generation by leveraging static-camera videos, unifying spatial and temporal controllability. Experimental results suggest LION-LoRA outperforms existing methods in trajectory control accuracy and motion strength adjustment with limited training data.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its systematic approach to LoRA fusion specifically for camera and object control in video diffusion. While LoRA is a well-established technique, the authors offer a detailed analysis of its behavior in the context of controllable video generation. The concepts of orthogonality in low-level layers and the importance of norm consistency across layers are interesting observations and provide a strong theoretical basis for the proposed method. The idea of a dedicated scaling token for linear control is a practical and seemingly effective addition. Extending this concept to 4D generation using static camera videos adds another layer of novelty.

*   **Significance:**  The paper addresses a significant problem in the field: the difficulty of precise control in video generation, particularly with limited data. If the claims of improved accuracy, stability, and generalization hold up, LION-LoRA could have a notable impact on downstream applications like interactive video editing and virtual environment simulation. A key strength is the improved stability and more controllable movement it provides.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper presents a clear and well-structured analysis of the limitations of standard LoRA fusion for fine-grained video control.
    *   The proposed principles (linearity, orthogonality, norm consistency) are theoretically justified and empirically validated.
    *   The scaling token mechanism is a simple yet effective way to achieve linear control over motion amplitudes.
    *   The experiments demonstrate promising results, showing superior performance compared to existing methods, including re-implemented versions of prior works for fair comparison.
    *   The training efficiency (4k iterations) is a major plus.
    *   The training-free fusion element is impressive.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the paper claims superior generalization, this aspect could be strengthened with more diverse datasets and scenarios. All experiments are based on DL3DV and the extension to other datasets and models could be studied further.
    *   The qualitative comparisons in Figure 9 reveal some visual differences but a more detailed visual analysis is lacking. Showing multiple examples from different categories may allow for more in-depth assessment of the algorithm and its capabilities.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is well-written and addresses a timely and relevant problem. The insights into LoRA fusion and the practical techniques proposed could inspire further research in controllable video generation. The parameter efficiency makes this approach easily applicable to a broader community. If the LION-LoRA approach proves robust and generalizable in future studies, it could become a standard technique for controlling video diffusion models.

*   **Overall:** The paper makes a solid contribution by analyzing and addressing the limitations of LoRA fusion for controllable video generation. The principles are well-justified, the method is practical, and the results are promising. While more extensive testing and generalization studies would strengthen the claims further, it's a significant advance in the field.

**Score: 7.8**

**Rationale:**

The score of 7.8 reflects the paper's solid contribution with clear novelty and a potentially high impact. It falls short of a higher score due primarily to the need for more rigorous testing and demonstrating broader generalizability of the method. While the ideas of orthogonality, norm consistency, and the scaling token are strong, the extent to which they generalize to significantly different datasets, models, and control paradigms remains somewhat open. Overall, the insights provided by the analysis and the potential impact from improved camera and motion control within diffusion models makes this paper a worthwhile contribution.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Smoothie-Qwen: Post-Hoc Smoothing to Reduce Language Bias in Multilingual LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05686v1)
- **Authors**: SeungWon Ji, Jungyup Lee, Jemin Kim, Sang Park, SeungJae Lee
- **Abstract**: Multilingual large language models (LLMs) often exhibit language confusion, a tendency to generate responses in a dominant language irrespective of the prompt's language. To address this, we propose Smoothie-Qwen, a lightweight, post-hoc method that mitigates language bias without retraining. This technique selectively adjusts token-level output probabilities to effectively suppress undesired language generation. Applied to the Qwen model, our method reduces unintended Chinese output by over 95% while preserving task accuracy on multilingual benchmarks. This work provides a practical and efficient solution for enhancing the language controllability of LLMs, making them more reliable for global applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Smoothie-Qwen: Post-Hoc Smoothing to Reduce Language Bias in Multilingual LLMs":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Smoothie-Qwen, a post-hoc method to mitigate language bias in multilingual large language models (LLMs) without retraining. The technique involves selectively adjusting token-level output probabilities to suppress the generation of undesired languages (specifically Chinese, in their experiments). This is achieved by assigning risk scores to tokens based on Unicode ranges and subword patterns, and then applying a non-linear smoothing function to the model's final output layer. Experiments on the Qwen model demonstrate that Smoothie-Qwen significantly reduces unintended Chinese output while preserving task accuracy on multilingual benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of post-hoc intervention to address language bias is not entirely new (as the Related Work section acknowledges). However, the specific implementation of Smoothie-Qwen, using a probabilistic smoothing approach based on token-level risk assessment and non-linear weight scaling, appears to be a novel contribution. The use of Unicode ranges and subword analysis to identify target tokens is a practical and straightforward approach. What differentiates this paper is the "soft suppression" strategy, allowing for pinyin pronunciation, which is useful in certain situations.
*   **Significance:** Language bias is a well-recognized problem in multilingual LLMs, and this paper offers a practical and efficient solution. The post-hoc nature of the method is a significant advantage, as it avoids the need for costly retraining. The fact that it can be applied to any publicly available model is also a major plus. By improving language controllability, Smoothie-Qwen contributes to making LLMs more reliable and trustworthy for global applications. The empirical results, showing a substantial reduction in unintended Chinese output with minimal impact on task accuracy, are convincing. Also, the qualitative analysis highlights the intelligent adaptions of the model, e.g. switching to Pinyin or English when Chinese is being suppressed.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Practicality:** The method is lightweight, training-free, and easily implementable.
    *   **Effectiveness:** The experiments demonstrate significant bias reduction with minimal performance degradation.
    *   **Clear Explanation:** The paper clearly explains the technical details of the method and provides a well-structured evaluation.
    *   **Thorough analysis**: In addition to quantitive analysis, the inclusion of the qualitative examples is important in verifying that the proposed changes make sense.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Static Application:** As mentioned in the limitations, the weight modification is context-insensitive. This limits its utility in cases where legitimate requests for the suppressed language are made.
    *   **Heuristic Risk Estimation:** The n-gram sampling method for fragmented tokens is heuristic. This leads to false positives and false negatives in its token recognition.
    *   **Manual Hyperparameter Tuning:** The need for manual tuning of the `min_scale` and `smoothness` parameters limits the "plug-and-play" convenience of the method.
    *   The results are demonstrated only on a Qwen model suppressing Chinese; more models and languages would bolster the results.
    *   The code example in Table 4 can lead to possible vulnerabilities due to the use of `ord()` to get character codes.

*   **Potential Influence:** Smoothie-Qwen has the potential to become a widely adopted technique for mitigating language bias in multilingual LLMs. Its simplicity and effectiveness make it an attractive option for developers and researchers. The method could also inspire further research into more sophisticated post-hoc intervention techniques.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and practical solution to an important problem in multilingual LLMs. The empirical results are convincing, and the method is relatively easy to implement. However, the limitations related to the static application, heuristic risk estimation, and manual hyperparameter tuning prevent it from achieving a higher score. While the overall contribution is significant, it would benefit from more sophisticated methods of improving the model's overall robustness. Despite these limitations, the paper provides a valuable contribution to the field and has the potential to influence future research.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Robust One-step Speech Enhancement via Consistency Distillation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05688v1)
- **Authors**: Liang Xu, Longfei Felix Yan, W. Bastiaan Kleijn
- **Abstract**: Diffusion models have shown strong performance in speech enhancement, but their real-time applicability has been limited by multi-step iterative sampling. Consistency distillation has recently emerged as a promising alternative by distilling a one-step consistency model from a multi-step diffusion-based teacher model. However, distilled consistency models are inherently biased towards the sampling trajectory of the teacher model, making them less robust to noise and prone to inheriting inaccuracies from the teacher model. To address this limitation, we propose ROSE-CD: Robust One-step Speech Enhancement via Consistency Distillation, a novel approach for distilling a one-step consistency model. Specifically, we introduce a randomized learning trajectory to improve the model's robustness to noise. Furthermore, we jointly optimize the one-step model with two time-domain auxiliary losses, enabling it to recover from teacher-induced errors and surpass the teacher model in overall performance. This is the first pure one-step consistency distillation model for diffusion-based speech enhancement, achieving 54 times faster inference speed and superior performance compared to its 30-step teacher model. Experiments on the VoiceBank-DEMAND dataset demonstrate that the proposed model achieves state-of-the-art performance in terms of speech quality. Moreover, its generalization ability is validated on both an out-of-domain dataset and real-world noisy recordings.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Robust One-step Speech Enhancement via Consistency Distillation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ROSE-CD, a novel approach for one-step speech enhancement based on consistency distillation from a pre-trained diffusion model.  It addresses the limitations of standard consistency distillation, which often inherits biases and inaccuracies from the teacher model, making it less robust to noise. ROSE-CD incorporates randomized trajectory learning during distillation to improve robustness.  Furthermore, it jointly optimizes the model with time-domain auxiliary losses (PESQ and SI-SDR) to encourage recovery from teacher-induced errors and enhance perceptual quality and temporal fidelity.  The results demonstrate a significant speed-up in inference compared to the teacher model and state-of-the-art performance on the VoiceBank-DEMAND dataset, along with strong generalization capabilities on out-of-domain datasets and real-world noisy recordings.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonable amount of novelty in the field of speech enhancement, specifically in the domain of diffusion-based models. The use of randomized trajectory learning during consistency distillation is a valuable contribution. It addresses a well-known limitation of existing consistency distillation methods, namely the reliance and imitation of possibly flawed teacher trajectories. The integration of auxiliary time-domain losses (PESQ and SI-SDR) within the consistency distillation framework provides a novel strategy to refine the one-step model and achieve better performance than the teacher model. Also, one-step speech enhancement via consistency distillation itself is a relatively recent direction in the domain, making the paper a worthwhile contribution to the recent studies.

*   **Significance:** The paper demonstrates the potential for consistency distillation to create highly efficient one-step speech enhancement models with improved robustness and performance compared to multi-step diffusion models. The results show a significant speed-up in inference time (54x) while achieving better or comparable speech quality. This has implications for real-time applications where computational resources and latency are critical constraints. The validation of generalization capabilities on out-of-domain data and real-world recordings further strengthens the practical significance of the research.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addresses a key limitation:** The paper directly tackles the problem of bias and lack of robustness in standard consistency distillation.
    *   **Effective method:** The proposed randomized trajectory learning and joint optimization strategy are effective in improving model performance and generalization.
    *   **State-of-the-art performance:** The model achieves state-of-the-art PESQ scores on the VoiceBank-DEMAND dataset.
    *   **Demonstrated Speedup:** Provides a significant speed advantage over the teacher model.
    *   **Rigorous evaluation:** The evaluation includes experiments on various datasets and metrics, providing a comprehensive assessment of the model's performance.
    *   **Clarity:** The methodology is well-explained and supported by experiments, making the paper easy to understand.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Teacher Model:** The consistency distillation approach is inherently dependent on the quality of the pre-trained teacher model. While the paper aims to mitigate this dependence, the performance of ROSE-CD is still limited by the capabilities of the teacher. A more robust pre-trained teacher model would allow for more room to improvement.
    *   **Complexity of Implementation:** Implementing consistency distillation, especially with randomized trajectories and joint optimization, can be complex and require careful tuning of hyperparameters. This might limit its adoption by researchers with limited resources or expertise in diffusion models.
    *   **Limited Comparative Analysis:** The paper could benefit from a more detailed comparison with other one-step speech enhancement methods, especially hybrid models that combine predictive and generative approaches. This would provide a clearer understanding of the advantages and disadvantages of ROSE-CD compared to alternative solutions.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in speech enhancement by highlighting the importance of robustness in consistency distillation and providing a practical method for achieving it. The combination of randomized trajectories and joint optimization could be applied to other generative modeling tasks beyond speech enhancement. The efficient one-step architecture could inspire the development of real-time speech enhancement systems for mobile devices and other resource-constrained platforms.
    * **Clarity:**
        * There is some ambiguity regarding the experimental setup, especially concerning the exact architecture and training details of the teacher model.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses of the paper, a score of 7 is warranted. The paper presents a reasonably novel approach to address a relevant problem in speech enhancement, demonstrating significant performance improvements and achieving state-of-the-art results. While the reliance on a teacher model and the complexity of implementation are limitations, the paper's contribution to the field of efficient and robust diffusion-based speech enhancement is valuable.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: eess.AS
- **Score**: 7/10

### Agentic-R1: Distilled Dual-Strategy Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05707v1)
- **Authors**: Weihua Du, Pranjal Aggarwal, Sean Welleck, Yiming Yang
- **Abstract**: Current long chain-of-thought (long-CoT) models excel at mathematical reasoning but rely on slow and error-prone natural language traces. Tool-augmented agents address arithmetic via code execution, but often falter on complex logical tasks. We introduce a fine-tuning framework, DualDistill, that distills complementary reasoning strategies from multiple teachers into a unified student model. Using this approach, we train Agentic-R1, which dynamically selects the optimal strategy for each query, invoking tools for arithmetic and algorithmic problems, and using text-based reasoning for abstract ones. Our method improves accuracy across a range of tasks, including both computation-intensive and standard benchmarks, demonstrating the effectiveness of multi-strategy distillation in achieving robust and efficient reasoning. Our project is available at https://github.com/StigLidu/DualDistill
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Agentic-R1: Distilled Dual-Strategy Reasoning":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces `Agentic-R1`, a language model fine-tuned with a novel framework called `DualDistill`, designed to combine the strengths of two distinct reasoning strategies: pure language-based reasoning (long-CoT) and tool-augmented reasoning (using code execution). `DualDistill` distills knowledge from two teacher models (one expert in each strategy) by composing solution trajectories based on their correctness. The resulting `Agentic-R1` dynamically selects the optimal strategy for each query, using tools for arithmetic and algorithmic tasks and text-based reasoning for abstract problems. The authors demonstrate improved accuracy across a range of mathematical tasks, including computationally intensive and standard benchmarks, suggesting the effectiveness of their multi-strategy distillation approach.  A final self-distillation step further refines the model's strategy selection.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the `DualDistill` framework for combining different reasoning strategies into a single model. While tool-augmented language models and knowledge distillation are established techniques, the specific method of trajectory composition from heterogeneous teachers based on solution correctness is a novel contribution. The idea of teaching a model to *choose* between reasoning approaches, rather than forcing a single method, is also significant.

*   **Significance:**  The paper addresses a key limitation of current long-CoT models (reliance on error-prone natural language) and tool-augmented agents (struggling with abstract reasoning). By combining these strategies, `Agentic-R1` achieves improved performance across a broader range of mathematical problems, suggesting the potential for more robust and efficient reasoning. The results on DeepMath-L and Combinatorics300, which benefit from both reasoning types, support this claim. The self-distillation step is also a nice addition as it attempts to mitigate the ability mismatch between student and teacher model.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Statement:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing approaches and motivates the need for a hybrid reasoning strategy.
    *   **Novel Method:** The `DualDistill` framework is a well-defined and innovative approach to combining different reasoning styles.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:**  The experiments demonstrate consistent improvements over competitive baselines on several challenging datasets.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** Demonstrates the effectiveness of trajectory composition.
    *   **Qualitative Examples:** The examples provide insights into the model's adaptive strategy switching behavior.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Manual Transition Design:** The transition words connecting different reasoning strategies are designed manually, which limits the naturalness and fluidity of the generated content. Automating this process could further improve the model's performance and readability.
    *   **Limited Training Data:** The training dataset size (2.6k examples) is relatively small, especially considering the complexity of the task. While sufficient for fine-tuning a model already pre-trained on both text and code, a larger dataset could enable more complex strategy learning. The performance drop on relatively simpler MATH500 task, relative to DeepSeek-R1-Distill-7B, indicates that there's room for improvement on the 'pure' reasoning front.
    *   **Reliance on Existing Models:** The approach depends on having access to strong teacher models for each strategy, which may not always be available. This could limit the applicability of the framework to other domains.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of more versatile and adaptive language agents. The idea of dynamically selecting between different reasoning strategies could be extended to other domains beyond mathematics. The `DualDistill` framework provides a valuable blueprint for combining diverse capabilities into a single model.

**Rigorous Rationale for the Score:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to a significant problem in the field of language model reasoning. The experimental results are compelling, and the ablation studies provide evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed method. While the limited training data and manual transition design represent weaknesses, the overall contribution is substantial.

Given the innovation in strategy distillation and compelling results, this work represents a significant contribution to the field, addressing a key limitation of current methods and opening avenues for more robust and flexible reasoning. Thus I assign a solid score of **7**.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### DRAGON: Dynamic RAG Benchmark On News
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05713v1)
- **Authors**: Fedor Chernogorskii, Sergei Averkiev, Liliya Kudraleeva, Zaven Martirosian, Maria Tikhonova, Valentin Malykh, Alena Fenogenova
- **Abstract**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) is a widely adopted approach for improving the factuality of large language models (LLMs) by incorporating external knowledge at inference time. Although there exist multiple RAG benchmarks for English, evaluation resources for other languages, including Russian, remain scarce and static, failing to capture the dynamic nature of real-world deployments. In this work, we present DRAGON (Dynamic RAG Benchmark On News), the first dynamic benchmark for evaluating RAG systems in Russian on a changing news corpora. DRAGON is built upon a regularly updated corpus of Russian news and public documents and supports comprehensive evaluation of both the retriever and generator components. Question generation is performed automatically with the use of Knowledge Graph constructed from the corpus and enables the extraction of four core question types aligned with distinct subgraph patterns. We release a complete evaluation framework comprising the pipeline for automatic question generation, evaluation scripts, which are potentially reusable for other languages and multilingual settings, and benchmark data. We also launch a public leaderboard to encourage community participation and comparison.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the DRAGON paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DRAGON (Dynamic RAG Benchmark On News), a novel, dynamic benchmark for evaluating Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems in the Russian language.  Unlike existing static benchmarks, DRAGON uses a regularly updated news corpus as its knowledge base, making it more reflective of real-world scenarios.  The paper details the benchmark's architecture, including data acquisition and processing, question generation via Knowledge Graph subgraphs, and evaluation framework.  It releases open-source code for question generation and evaluation, and a public leaderboard for tracking performance. The authors conduct baseline experiments with several LLMs and embedding models, evaluating both retrieval and generation performance. The question set consists of simple, set, multi-hop, and conditional question types.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the dynamic nature of the benchmark and its focus on the Russian language. Existing RAG benchmarks are predominantly in English and often static. DRAGON fills a gap by providing a resource that simulates the constant evolution of information in real-world applications. The automatic question generation from Knowledge Graph subgraphs, while not entirely new as a concept, is adapted to the Russian language news domain and tailored to specific question types.  The release of the open-source evaluation framework is a positive contribution.

*   **Significance:** A dynamic benchmark is crucial for evaluating RAG systems meant to work with time-sensitive information. This is important for applications like news analysis, customer support, and any other domain where knowledge changes rapidly. Having a well-designed benchmark in Russian will encourage research and development in this language, which is underrepresented in the NLP community compared to English. The open-source nature of the benchmark makes it easier for other researchers to adopt, extend, and adapt to their specific needs. Furthermore, The performance results presented in the paper highlight the effectiveness of various LLMs and embedding models when paired in RAG frameworks.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Dynamic Data Source:**  Utilizing a regularly updated news corpus is a major strength, addressing the limitations of static benchmarks.
    *   **Open-Source Framework:** The release of code, data, and a leaderboard fosters reproducibility and community participation.
    *   **Question Diversity:** The variety of question types (simple, set, multi-hop, conditional) allows for more comprehensive evaluation of RAG capabilities.
    *   **Russian Language Focus:** This fills an important gap in the RAG benchmark landscape.
    *   The human evaluation of the questions generated for the data set is commendable, ensuring reasonable quality and semantic meaning.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Domain Limitation:** The benchmark is currently focused solely on the news domain. While relevant, extending it to other domains in the future would increase its broader applicability.
    *   **Evaluation Metrics:**  The paper acknowledges the limitations of surface-level evaluation metrics like ROUGE and advocates the role of LLMs for evaluation as well. Developing more nuanced and human-aligned evaluation metrics, or a thorough justification of existing metric selection for this data would improve the analysis.
    *   **Limited Baseline Experiments:**  While baseline experiments are presented, a more extensive comparison with a wider range of architectures (including state-of-the-art RAG approaches) would further strengthen the paper's impact.
    *   The paper could include a more detailed analysis of the types of errors that RAG systems make on the benchmark. This could inform future research directions.
    *   The source of news data (while ethical and responsible, with RAMBLER) could introduce potential bias in generated questions. The scope and mitigation efforts need to be analyzed.

*   **Potential Influence:** DRAGON has the potential to significantly influence the development and evaluation of RAG systems in Russian and potentially other languages. It provides a valuable resource for researchers to compare and improve their models. The open-source nature and the public leaderboard will further facilitate community contributions and accelerate progress. The design choices (automatic question generation from KGs) will be of interest to the broader RAG community.

**Justification of Score:**

DRAGON represents a solid and valuable contribution to the RAG evaluation landscape. While the core components (KG-based question generation, open-source release) are not entirely groundbreaking on their own, their combination within a dynamic and Russian-language context provides significant practical value. The limitations, while present, do not diminish the overall utility of the benchmark.

Score: **7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### HIRAG: Hierarchical-Thought Instruction-Tuning Retrieval-Augmented Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05714v1)
- **Authors**: YiHan Jiao, ZheHao Tan, Dan Yang, DuoLin Sun, Jie Feng, Jian Wang, Peng Wei
- **Abstract**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) has become a fundamental paradigm for addressing the challenges faced by large language models in handling real-time information and domain-specific problems. Traditional RAG systems primarily rely on the in-context learning (ICL) capabilities of the large language model itself. Still, in-depth research on the specific capabilities needed by the RAG generation model is lacking, leading to challenges with inconsistent document quality and retrieval system imperfections. Even the limited studies that fine-tune RAG generative models often \textit{lack a granular focus on RAG task} or \textit{a deeper utilization of chain-of-thought processes}. To address this, we propose that RAG models should possess three progressively hierarchical abilities (1) Filtering: the ability to select relevant information; (2) Combination: the ability to combine semantic information across paragraphs; and (3) RAG-specific reasoning: the ability to further process external knowledge using internal knowledge. Thus, we introduce our new RAG instruction fine-tuning method, Hierarchical-Thought Instruction-Tuning Retrieval-Augmented Generation (HIRAG) incorporates a "think before answering" strategy. This method enhances the model's open-book examination capability by utilizing multi-level progressive chain-of-thought. Experiments show that the HIRAG training strategy significantly improves the model's performance on datasets such as RGB, PopQA, MuSiQue, HotpotQA, and PubmedQA.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "HIRAG: Hierarchical-Thought Instruction-Tuning Retrieval-Augmented Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces HIRAG, a novel instruction-tuning method for Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) models.  HIRAG aims to improve RAG performance by focusing on three progressively hierarchical abilities: filtering (selecting relevant information), combination (integrating information from multiple sources), and RAG-specific reasoning (processing external knowledge using internal knowledge). The core idea is to use a "think before answering" strategy, implemented through a multi-level progressive chain-of-thought (CoT) approach.  The method is evaluated on various datasets, including RGB, PopQA, MuSiQue, HotpotQA, and PubMedQA, demonstrating significant performance gains compared to existing RAG methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The concept of hierarchical abilities (filtering, combination, reasoning) is a reasonable and intuitively appealing way to decompose the RAG task. The "think before answering" through a progressive CoT approach is a valuable technique for improving the model's reasoning process and open-book capabilities. The specific task-specific CoT paradigms for filtering, combination, and RAG-specific reasoning seem to be well designed for their respective capabilities.

*   **Significance:** RAG is a crucial area of research in addressing the limitations of large language models (LLMs) in knowledge-intensive tasks. This paper contributes to the field by offering a structured approach to instruction-tuning RAG models, leading to tangible improvements in performance. The gains are especially noteworthy considering the model size is kept smaller than other higher-performing base models (e.g., GPT-4). By designing specific training tasks for each of the 3 capabilities, the paper also addresses the issue of lacking focus on granular RAG tasks, one of the issues it has called out in the Introduction section.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of traditional RAG approaches and motivates the need for more granular and task-specific training.
    *   **Well-Defined Methodology:** HIRAG is a well-defined and clearly explained method, encompassing the hierarchical abilities, progressive CoT, and training strategy.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents a thorough experimental evaluation across various datasets, including benchmark, open-domain, and domain-specific tasks.
    *   **Significant Performance Gains:** The results demonstrate that HIRAG achieves significant improvements over existing RAG methods and performs competitively with larger models in some scenarios.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies provide evidence for the contribution of each hierarchical ability to the overall performance of HIRAG.
    *   **Case Study:** The case study illustrates how the model works in practice and demonstrate HIRAG's effectiveness over baseline models.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Implementation Details:** Though a thorough explanation is given on the training process for HIRAG, a significant section of it relies on Appendices A.1/A.2/B, making it difficult to fully understand and evaluate the implementation without reviewing the appendices and the actual code. The paper also could be improved with a more thorough discussion of the computational cost and resources required for training.
    *   **Heavy Dependence on Documents**: This limitation calls out a dependence on well-formatted, thorough references, which doesn't consider cases of sparse, incomplete, or ambiguous data, which makes the framework's robustness for broader applicability a limitation of the paper.

* **Potential Influence**: The paper's structured approach to instruction-tuning RAG models and its emphasis on hierarchical abilities could inspire future research directions in the field, potentially leading to even more effective RAG systems. The task-specific CoT training strategy, combined with progressive learning, has broader applicability within LLM instruction tuning, too.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper offers a valuable contribution to the RAG field with its hierarchical approach and improved instruction-tuning strategy, it's not entirely revolutionary. It builds upon existing CoT and RAG techniques but presents a novel and effective combination that leads to measurable performance gains. Given the comprehensiveness of the evaluation and the clear demonstration of improvements, but keeping in mind the dependence on high-quality structured data and some lack of transparency in the implementation details, a score of 7 is justified.

**Score: 7**
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Large Language Models for Agent-Based Modelling: Current and possible uses across the modelling cycle
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05723v1)
- **Authors**: Loïs Vanhée, Melania Borit, Peer-Olaf Siebers, Roger Cremades, Christopher Frantz, Önder Gürcan, František Kalvas, Denisa Reshef Kera, Vivek Nallur, Kavin Narasimhan, Martin Neumann
- **Abstract**: The emergence of Large Language Models (LLMs) with increasingly sophisticated natural language understanding and generative capabilities has sparked interest in the Agent-based Modelling (ABM) community. With their ability to summarize, generate, analyze, categorize, transcribe and translate text, answer questions, propose explanations, sustain dialogue, extract information from unstructured text, and perform logical reasoning and problem-solving tasks, LLMs have a good potential to contribute to the modelling process. After reviewing the current use of LLMs in ABM, this study reflects on the opportunities and challenges of the potential use of LLMs in ABM. It does so by following the modelling cycle, from problem formulation to documentation and communication of model results, and holding a critical stance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the use of Large Language Models (LLMs) in agent-based modelling (ABM). It provides a review of existing studies in this emerging area and then proposes a structured approach for considering LLMs' potential contributions across the entire ABM modelling cycle. This cycle includes problem formulation, system analysis, conceptualization, implementation, verification, validation, and interpretation/communication of results. For each phase, the paper discusses *why* the phase is important, *how* LLMs can assist, potential *pitfalls*, and *how to mitigate* these pitfalls. The authors emphasize that LLMs should augment, not replace, human expertise and critical thinking. The conclusion highlights the importance of recognizing the fundamental differences between ABMs (rule-based) and LLMs (pattern-based) and using LLMs to enrich and challenge, not simply automate, the modelling process.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Scope:** The paper provides a valuable and structured overview of how LLMs can be integrated into *every* phase of the ABM modelling cycle. This is a significant improvement over existing literature that often focuses on specific aspects, such as agent behavior generation.
    *   **Pragmatic Approach:** The authors don't blindly advocate for LLM integration. The "pitfalls and mitigation" sections for each phase are particularly valuable, highlighting the risks of bias, oversimplification, and loss of transparency. The emphasis on human oversight is crucial.
    *   **Clear Structure and Readability:** The paper is well-organized and clearly written. The 'Why? How? Pitfalls? Mitigation?' framework makes it easy to understand the potential benefits and challenges of LLM use in each modelling phase.
    *   **Timeliness and Relevance:** The topic is highly relevant given the rapid advances in LLM technology and the increasing interest in applying them to social simulation.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Empirical Validation:** The paper is primarily theoretical. While it references existing studies, it doesn't present new empirical evidence demonstrating the effectiveness of LLMs in specific ABM projects. This limits the paper's practical impact. The "results and discussion" section focuses on the literature review but offers little concrete evaluation of LLMs in ABM other than listing existing uses.
    *   **Depth of Exploration:** While comprehensive, the paper's coverage of each phase is necessarily brief. More in-depth case studies or examples would strengthen the analysis. For example, showing specific prompts or coding examples for LLM-assisted code generation or documentation would add considerable value.
    *   **Lack of a Strong Novelty Argument:** The paper convincingly presents the usefulness of LLMs in the ABM pipeline. However, at the end, when it tries to make a stronger claim about LLMs "enriching" ABM by challenging existing conceptualizations, it somewhat falls flat. This idea isn't really fleshed out.
    *   **Over-reliance on LLMs as language tools:** The paper largely frames LLMs as tools for processing and generating language, when they can also be seen as knowledge bases or systems capable of reasoning. More exploration of these capabilities, which extend beyond language, would strengthen the paper.

*   **Novelty and Significance:** The paper makes a solid contribution by providing a structured framework for thinking about LLM integration in ABM. The comprehensive approach and the emphasis on potential pitfalls are particularly valuable. However, the lack of empirical validation and the limited depth of exploration somewhat diminish its impact. The paper's main strength lies in its systematic approach, bringing a critical perspective to a field that's often driven by hype. While not groundbreaking, the paper provides a solid foundation for future research and development in this area. It provides a solid, albeit theoretical, basis for a comprehensive pipeline approach.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper is a well-organized and insightful contribution to a rapidly evolving field. It provides a valuable framework for thinking about LLM integration in ABM and raises important caveats. However, its lack of empirical validation and limited depth of exploration prevent it from being a truly transformative work. The reliance on LLMs as primarily language tools, rather than systems capable of reasoning, also limits its score.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.MA
- **Score**: 7/10

### ContextASR-Bench: A Massive Contextual Speech Recognition Benchmark
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05727v1)
- **Authors**: He Wang, Linhan Ma, Dake Guo, Xiong Wang, Lei Xie, Jin Xu, Junyang Lin
- **Abstract**: Automatic Speech Recognition (ASR) has been extensively investigated, yet prior evaluative efforts have largely been restricted to contextless paradigms. This constraint stems from the limited proficiency of conventional ASR models in context modeling and their deficiency in memory and reasoning based on world knowledge. Recent breakthroughs in the development of Large Language Models (LLMs) and corresponding Large Audio Language Models (LALMs) have markedly enhanced the visibility of general artificial intelligence capabilities. Consequently, there exists a compelling need for a benchmark that can evaluate both the generality and intelligence of ASR systems. To address this gap, we propose ContextASR-Bench: a comprehensive, large-scale benchmark designed to assess contextual speech recognition. This benchmark encompasses up to 40,000 data entries across over 10 domains, enabling a thorough evaluation of model performance in scenarios that omit or incorporate coarse-grained or fine-grained contextual information. Moreover, diverging from conventional ASR evaluations, our benchmark includes an analysis of model efficacy in recognizing named entities mentioned within the auditory input. Our extensive evaluation highlights that LALMs, with strong world knowledge and context learning capabilities, outperform conventional ASR models by a large margin. The dataset and evaluation code have been released at https://github.com/MrSupW/ContextASR-Bench.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the ContextASR-Bench paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ContextASR-Bench, a new benchmark designed to evaluate contextual speech recognition. It addresses the limitations of existing ASR benchmarks that primarily focus on context-less scenarios, hindering the assessment of Large Language Models (LLMs) and Large Audio Language Models (LALMs) that leverage world knowledge and contextual understanding. The benchmark includes approximately 40,000 data entries across diverse domains. It offers three evaluation settings: contextless, coarse-grained context, and fine-grained context. It also introduces novel metrics like Named Entity WER (NE-WER) and Named Entity False Negative Rate (NE-FNR) to assess the accuracy of recognizing named entities. The paper demonstrates that LALMs significantly outperform conventional ASR models on ContextASR-Bench, highlighting the importance of world knowledge and context learning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary contribution lies in the **creation of a large-scale, multi-domain benchmark specifically designed for contextual ASR**. While individual components like using LLMs for data generation and zero-shot TTS are not entirely novel, their integration into a comprehensive evaluation framework for ASR is innovative. The use of NER datasets as a seed to generate colloquial, context-rich text and the inclusion of NE-WER and NE-FNR metrics is a good choice. The benchmark offers a way to move beyond limited-domain, contextless evaluation, and the inclusion of dialogue is also valuable.

*   **Significance:** The benchmark fills a crucial gap in the ASR evaluation landscape. With the rapid advancements in LALMs, there is a pressing need to measure their capabilities in real-world scenarios where context plays a vital role. ContextASR-Bench provides a standardized platform for comparing models and identifying areas for improvement. The dataset is freely available on Github. The results presented highlight the need to move beyond WER as a single measure for assessing models, particularly in knowledge-intensive ASR. The effort will likely spur further research into designing ASR systems capable of effectively utilizing contextual information.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Large-scale and multi-domain: The benchmark covers a wide range of topics, making it more representative of real-world scenarios.
    *   Context-aware evaluation: The three evaluation settings allow for a fine-grained assessment of model performance under varying levels of contextual information.
    *   Named entity-focused metrics: NE-WER and NE-FNR provide valuable insights into a model's ability to recognize and process domain-specific terms and entities.
    *   Thorough experiments: Comprehensive experiments are conducted across different existing and LLM based ASR systems.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Synthetic speech: The use of synthetic speech instead of real recordings raises concerns about acoustic authenticity and diversity, which might not fully capture the complexities of real-world speech. While zero-shot TTS has improved, it still has limitations.
    *   English and Mandarin only: The benchmark is currently limited to English and Mandarin, restricting its applicability to other languages.
    *   Potential for prompt engineering bias: As with any LLM-based evaluation, there is a possibility that the prompt design could inadvertently favor certain models or encourage undesirable behaviors (e.g. hallucinations in the fine-grained setting as demonstrated in the paper).
    *   Text normalization: It should be mentioned in more details whether it applies to the generation stage by LLM or just normalization is used during speech transcription for the G2P module.

*   **Impact:** The release of this benchmark will have a significant impact on the ASR research community. It will facilitate the development of more robust and context-aware ASR systems, accelerating the progress of speech recognition technology. It is likely that many researchers will use this as a standardized dataset for training and fine tuning models in the future.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

ContextASR-Bench addresses a relevant gap in ASR evaluation and provides a strong foundation for future research in this area. The integration of LLM-driven data generation with ASR provides an innovative way to obtain speech data. However, the limitations associated with the synthetic data is a major issue. Thus, a score of 7 represents a valuable, above average contribution that will likely see some impact, however, it doesn't reach the level of paradigm-shifting benchmark due to the weakness associated with synthetic data.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: eess.AS
- **Score**: 7/10

### Non-Intrusive Binaural Speech Intelligibility Prediction Using Mamba for Hearing-Impaired Listeners
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05729v1)
- **Authors**: Katsuhiko Yamamoto, Koichi Miyazaki
- **Abstract**: Speech intelligibility prediction (SIP) models have been used as objective metrics to assess intelligibility for hearing-impaired (HI) listeners. In the Clarity Prediction Challenge 2 (CPC2), non-intrusive binaural SIP models based on transformers showed high prediction accuracy. However, the self-attention mechanism theoretically incurs high computational and memory costs, making it a bottleneck for low-latency, power-efficient devices. This may also degrade the temporal processing of binaural SIPs. Therefore, we propose Mamba-based SIP models instead of transformers for the temporal processing blocks. Experimental results show that our proposed SIP model achieves competitive performance compared to the baseline while maintaining a relatively small number of parameters. Our analysis suggests that the SIP model based on bidirectional Mamba effectively captures contextual and spatial speech information from binaural signals.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a Mamba-based speech intelligibility prediction (SIP) model for hearing-impaired (HI) listeners. It aims to replace the transformer-based temporal processing blocks in existing non-intrusive binaural SIP models with Mamba blocks to reduce computational costs and memory usage, addressing limitations for deployment on low-latency, power-efficient devices like hearing aids. The authors evaluate the performance of their Mamba-based model against a transformer-based baseline (E011) using the Clarity Prediction Challenge 2 (CPC2) dataset. The results indicate that the proposed model achieves comparable or slightly better performance with fewer parameters, particularly the bi-directional Mamba architecture. The paper also investigates the impact of varying the temporal pooling size of Whisper features on SI prediction.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the application of Mamba, a relatively new state-space sequence model, to the task of binaural SIP for HI listeners. Replacing transformers with Mamba for temporal processing within the SIP framework represents a reasonable adaptation of a recent architectural advance. While the core SIP framework remains largely unchanged from the E011 baseline, the Mamba substitution is a non-trivial engineering effort with potentially significant practical implications.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from the potential to develop more efficient and deployable SIP models for HI listeners. The theoretical advantages of Mamba over transformers—linear time complexity and constant memory during inference—are particularly appealing for embedded devices like hearing aids, where resource constraints are significant.
